{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01HzQHt4drUABqwaY4vcoqNL","type":"tool_result","content":"339\tdef _check_control(tools_dir: Path) -> DoctorCheck:\n340\t    \"\"\"Plan 032 Faz 032e — an operator pause is health information, not illness.\"\"\"\n341\t    from .control import effective_control\n342\t\n343\t    state = effective_control(tools_dir)\n344\t    if state.paused_all:\n345\t        return DoctorCheck(\"control\", \"warn\", \"executor_paused\", state.to_dict())\n346\t    return DoctorCheck(\"control\", \"ok\", \"\", state.to_dict())\n347\t\n348\t\n349\tdef _check_notifications(tools_dir: Path) -> DoctorCheck:\n350\t    \"\"\"Plan 032 Faz 032e — a channel that keeps failing means nobody hears.\"\"\"\n351\t    from .notify import configured_channels, read_outbox\n352\t\n353\t    rows = read_outbox(tools_dir)\n354\t    recent = rows[-50:]\n355\t    failed = [r for r in recent if r.get(\"status\") == \"failed\"]\n356\t    detail = {\"configured_channels\": list(configured_channels()), \"recent_rows\": len(recent), \"recent_failed\": len(failed)}\n357\t    if failed:\n358\t        return DoctorCheck(\"notifications\", \"warn\", f\"recent_failures:{len(failed)}\", detail)\n359\t    return DoctorCheck(\"notifications\", \"ok\", \"\" if detail[\"configured_channels\"] else \"no_channel_configured\", detail)\n360\t\n361\t\n362\tdef _check_gateway(tools_dir: Path) -> DoctorCheck:\n363\t    \"\"\"Plan 032 Faz 032f — the droplet daemon's inbox and last beat, as\n364\t    information. Absent = not deployed on this host. Freshness is NOT judged\n365\t    here: `gateway_heartbeat_fresh` owns it, with a FAIL — this organ's\n366\t    `warn` on a four-day-old beat is how the dead daemon went unnoticed.\"\"\"\n367\t    import json\n368\t    from datetime import datetime, timezone\n369\t\n370\t    from .gateway.inbox import inbox_summary\n371\t    from .gateway.server import HEARTBEAT_RELPATH\n372\t\n373\t    path = tools_dir.joinpath(*HEARTBEAT_RELPATH)\n374\t    inbox = inbox_summary(tools_dir)\n375\t    if not path.exists():\n376\t        return DoctorCheck(\"gateway\", \"ok\", \"gateway_not_running_here\", {\"inbox\": inbox})\n377\t    try:\n378\t        beat = json.loads(path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n379\t        stamp = datetime.fromisoformat(str(beat.get(\"recorded_at\")).replace(\"Z\", \"+00:00\"))\n380\t    except (OSError, ValueError):\n381\t        return DoctorCheck(\"gateway\", \"warn\", \"heartbeat_unreadable\", {\"inbox\": inbox})\n382\t    age = (datetime.now(timezone.utc) - stamp).total_seconds()\n383\t    detail = {\"heartbeat_age_seconds\": int(age), \"inbox\": inbox, \"last_ran\": beat.get(\"ran\")}\n384\t    if inbox[\"pending\"] > 50:\n385\t        return DoctorCheck(\"gateway\", \"warn\", f\"inbox_backlog:{inbox['pending']}\", detail)\n386\t    return DoctorCheck(\"gateway\", \"ok\", \"\", detail)\n387\t\n388\t\n389\t# How many consecutive beats the daemon may miss before the doctor calls it\n390\t# dead. Five: one beat is a loaded host, two a slow tick; five in a row at\n391\t# the 60 s default is the same 300 s the first gateway organ warned at —\n392\t# except this organ FAILS, because a dead daemon is not information.\n393\tHEARTBEAT_STALE_AFTER_BEATS = 5\n394\t\n395\t\n396\tdef _check_gateway_heartbeat_fresh(tools_dir: Path, *, stale_after_beats: int = HEARTBEAT_STALE_AFTER_BEATS) -> DoctorCheck:\n397\t    \"\"\"B6 — the gateway daemon is alive, or the doctor is unhealthy.\n398\t\n399\t    CONFIRMED LIVE 2026-09-12: aria-gateway.service last beat 2026-09-08 and\n400\t    sat disabled/dead for four days while the doctor stayed healthy (the\n401\t    `gateway` organ warned, `healthy` counts only fails, and\n402\t    `self_improvement.scan_signals` lifts only fails). Staleness is counted\n403\t    in BEATS of the cadence the heartbeat itself declares\n404\t    (`poll_interval_seconds`, written by `scheduler.tick`); a beat from\n405\t    before that field existed is judged at the daemon default. An ABSENT\n406\t    heartbeat fails exactly when a schedule table exists — the table is what\n407\t    says a daemon is expected on this store; with no table this host never\n408\t    ran one, which is a fact rather than an illness.\n409\t    \"\"\"\n410\t    import json\n411\t    from datetime import datetime, timezone\n412\t\n413\t    from .gateway.daemon import DEFAULT_POLL_INTERVAL_SECONDS\n414\t    from .gateway.scheduler import fold_schedules\n415\t    from .gateway.server import HEARTBEAT_RELPATH\n416\t\n417\t    schedules = sorted(fold_schedules(tools_dir))\n418\t    path = tools_dir.joinpath(*HEARTBEAT_RELPATH)\n419\t    if not path.exists():\n420\t        if schedules:\n421\t            return DoctorCheck(\"gateway_heartbeat_fresh\", \"fail\", \"gateway_heartbeat_absent_with_schedules\", {\"schedules\": schedules})\n422\t        return DoctorCheck(\"gateway_heartbeat_fresh\", \"ok\", \"gateway_not_deployed_here\", {\"schedules\": []})\n423\t    try:\n424\t        beat = json.loads(path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n425\t        stamp = datetime.fromisoformat(str(beat.get(\"recorded_at\")).replace(\"Z\", \"+00:00\"))\n426\t        interval = float(beat.get(\"poll_interval_seconds\") or DEFAULT_POLL_INTERVAL_SECONDS)\n427\t    except (OSError, ValueError, TypeError, AttributeError):\n428\t        status = \"fail\" if schedules else \"warn\"\n429\t        return DoctorCheck(\"gateway_heartbeat_fresh\", status, \"gateway_heartbeat_unreadable\", {\"schedules\": schedules})\n430\t    age = (datetime.now(timezone.utc) - stamp).total_seconds()\n431\t    missed = age / interval if interval > 0 else float(\"inf\")\n432\t    detail = {\"heartbeat_age_seconds\": int(age), \"poll_interval_seconds\": interval, \"missed_beats\": round(missed, 1),\n433\t              \"stale_after_beats\": stale_after_beats, \"schedules\": schedules}\n434\t    if missed > stale_after_beats:\n435\t        return DoctorCheck(\"gateway_heartbeat_fresh\", \"fail\", f\"gateway_heartbeat_stale:{int(age)}s>{stale_after_beats}x{interval:g}s\", detail)\n436\t    return DoctorCheck(\"gateway_heartbeat_fresh\", \"ok\", \"\", detail)\n437\t\n438\t\n439\tdef _check_orchestrator(tools_dir: Path) -> DoctorCheck:\n440\t    \"\"\"A streak of ``cycle_failed`` exits is a FAULT, not a series of bad\n441\t    nights. The orchestrator fails closed on a failed cycle — nothing behind\n442\t    it (planner dispatch, the convergence drainer) runs — so a store whose\n443\t    last runs all died that way has not planned anything, whatever the other\n444\t    organs say. Four such runs in a row on the live store (2026-08-21 →\n445\t    2026-09-04) read as healthy here before this organ existed.\"\"\"\n446\t    from .orchestrator_exit_history import read_orchestrator_exit_streak\n447\t\n448\t    streak = read_orchestrator_exit_streak(tools_dir)\n449\t    detail = streak.to_dict()\n450\t    if streak.all_failed:\n451\t        return DoctorCheck(\n452\t            \"orchestrator\", \"fail\",\n453\t            f\"orchestrator_exits_all_cycle_failed:{len(streak.exits)}\", detail,\n454\t        )\n455\t    if streak.last_failed:\n456\t        return DoctorCheck(\"orchestrator\", \"warn\", \"last_orchestrator_exit_cycle_failed\", detail)\n457\t    if len(streak.exits) < 2:\n458\t        return DoctorCheck(\"orchestrator\", \"ok\", \"insufficient_history\", detail)\n459\t    return DoctorCheck(\"orchestrator\", \"ok\", \"\", detail)\n460\t\n461\t\n462\tdef _check_tools(tools_dir: Path) -> DoctorCheck:\n463\t    \"\"\"ARIA-HIGH-098 — the adapters that are not answering, by name and class.\n464\t\n465\t    A non-ok tool run no longer fails the night (``cycle_runtime_status``),\n466\t    which is exactly why it needs an organ: a degraded tool used to announce\n467\t    itself by killing the cycle, and now announces itself here. A streak that\n468\t    reached ``TOOL_DEGRADATION_HUMAN_REQUIRED_STREAK`` is a FAIL (a record is\n469\t    open for an operator); a shorter streak is a WARN naming the class and\n470\t    the count. A QUARANTINED tool's streak counts the cycles it has sat out\n471\t    (``tool_sit_out``: class ``quarantined``), so a tool ``tool_health``\n472\t    benched reaches FAIL on the same third night as one that crashes —\n473\t    quarantine is ``tool_health`` doing its job, but a tool out of the roster\n474\t    until an operator un-quarantines it is not something a healthy readout\n475\t    may be silent about; a quarantine with no cycle behind it yet is still\n476\t    named (``tools_quarantined``).\"\"\"\n477\t    from .tool_degradation import degradation_report\n478\t\n479\t    report = degradation_report(tools_dir)\n480\t    detail = {\n481\t        \"degraded\": report[\"degraded\"],\n482\t        \"quarantined\": report[\"quarantined\"],\n483\t        \"human_required_streak\": report[\"human_required_streak\"],\n484\t    }\n485\t    escalated = [entry[\"tool_id\"] for entry in report[\"degraded\"] if entry[\"human_required\"]]\n486\t    if escalated:\n487\t        return DoctorCheck(\"tools\", \"fail\", f\"tools_degraded_human_required:{','.join(escalated)}\", detail)\n488\t    if report[\"degraded\"]:\n489\t        named = \",\".join(\n490\t            f\"{entry['tool_id']}={entry['degradation_class']}x{entry['consecutive_cycles']}\"\n491\t            for entry in report[\"degraded\"]\n492\t        )\n493\t        return DoctorCheck(\"tools\", \"warn\", f\"tools_degraded:{named}\", detail)\n494\t    if report[\"quarantined\"]:\n495\t        return DoctorCheck(\"tools\", \"warn\", f\"tools_quarantined:{','.join(report['quarantined'])}\", detail)\n496\t    return DoctorCheck(\"tools\", \"ok\", \"\", detail)\n497\t\n498\t\n499\tdef _check_economy(tools_dir: Path) -> DoctorCheck:\n500\t    \"\"\"Plan 032 Faz 032i — a standing effort downgrade is information; no accepted\n501\t    result across a busy agent is a warning.\"\"\"\n502\t    from .token_economy import read_recommendations, usage_per_accepted_result\n503\t\n504\t    stats = usage_per_accepted_result(base_dir=tools_dir)\n505\t    downgrades = [f\"{r['target_agent']}/{r['role']}\" for r in read_recommendations(tools_dir) if r.get(\"kind\") == \"effort\" and r.get(\"action\") == \"downgrade\"]\n506\t    starved = [f\"{s.target_agent}/{s.role}\" for s in stats if s.spawns >= 5 and s.accepted == 0]\n507\t    detail = {\"stats\": [s.to_dict() for s in stats][:20], \"downgrades\": downgrades[-5:], \"starved\": starved}\n508\t    if starved:\n509\t        return DoctorCheck(\"economy\", \"warn\", f\"spawns_without_accepted_result:{len(starved)}\", _check_control(tools_dir: Path) -> DoctorCheck:\n    \"\"\"Plan 032 Faz 032e — an operator pause is health information, not illness.\"\"\"\n    from .control import effective_control\n\n    state = effective_control(tools_dir)\n    if state.paused_all:\n        return DoctorCheck(\"control\", \"warn\", \"executor_paused\", state.to_dict())\n    return DoctorCheck(\"control\", \"ok\", \"\", state.to_dict())\n\n\ndef _check_notifications(tools_dir: Path) -> DoctorCheck:\n    \"\"\"Plan 032 Faz 032e — a channel that keeps failing means nobody hears.\"\"\"\n    from .notify import configured_channels, read_outbox\n\n    rows = read_outbox(tools_dir)\n    recent = rows[-50:]\n    failed = [r for r in recent if r.get(\"status\") == \"failed\"]\n    detail = {\"configured_channels\": list(configured_channels()), \"recent_rows\": len(recent), \"recent_failed\": len(failed)}\n    if failed:\n        return DoctorCheck(\"notifications\", \"warn\", f\"recent_failures:{len(failed)}\", detail)\n    return DoctorCheck(\"notifications\", \"ok\", \"\" if detail[\"configured_channels\"] else \"no_channel_configured\", detail)\n\n\ndef _check_gateway(tools_dir: Path) -> DoctorCheck:\n    \"\"\"Plan 032 Faz 032f — the droplet daemon's inbox and last beat, as\n    information. Absent = not deployed on this host. Freshness is NOT judged\n    here: `gateway_heartbeat_fresh` owns it, with a FAIL — this organ's\n    `warn` on a four-day-old beat is how the dead daemon went unnoticed.\"\"\"\n    import json\n    from datetime import datetime, timezone\n\n    from .gateway.inbox import inbox_summary\n    from .gateway.server import HEARTBEAT_RELPATH\n\n    path = tools_dir.joinpath(*HEARTBEAT_RELPATH)\n    inbox = inbox_summary(tools_dir)\n    if not path.exists():\n        return DoctorCheck(\"gateway\", \"ok\", \"gateway_not_running_here\", {\"inbox\": inbox})\n    try:\n        beat = json.loads(path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n        stamp = datetime.fromisoformat(str(beat.get(\"recorded_at\")).replace(\"Z\", \"+00:00\"))\n    except (OSError, ValueError):\n        return DoctorCheck(\"gateway\", \"warn\", \"heartbeat_unreadable\", {\"inbox\": inbox})\n    age = (datetime.now(timezone.utc) - stamp).total_seconds()\n    detail = {\"heartbeat_age_seconds\": int(age), \"inbox\": inbox, \"last_ran\": beat.get(\"ran\")}\n    if inbox[\"pending\"] > 50:\n        return DoctorCheck(\"gateway\", \"warn\", f\"inbox_backlog:{inbox['pending']}\", detail)\n    return DoctorCheck(\"gateway\", \"ok\", \"\", detail)\n\n\n# How many consecutive beats the daemon may miss before the doctor calls it\n# dead. Five: one beat is a loaded host, two a slow tick; five in a row at\n# the 60 s default is the same 300 s the first gateway organ warned at —\n# except this organ FAILS, because a dead daemon is not information.\nHEARTBEAT_STALE_AFTER_BEATS = 5\n\n\ndef _check_gateway_heartbeat_fresh(tools_dir: Path, *, stale_after_beats: int = HEARTBEAT_STALE_AFTER_BEATS) -> DoctorCheck:\n    \"\"\"B6 — the gateway daemon is alive, or the doctor is unhealthy.\n\n    CONFIRMED LIVE 2026-09-12: aria-gateway.service last beat 2026-09-08 and\n    sat disabled/dead for four days while the doctor stayed healthy (the\n    `gateway` organ warned, `healthy` counts only fails, and\n    `self_improvement.scan_signals` lifts only fails). Staleness is counted\n    in BEATS of the cadence the heartbeat itself declares\n    (`poll_interval_seconds`, written by `scheduler.tick`); a beat from\n    before that field existed is judged at the daemon default. An ABSENT\n    heartbeat fails exactly when a schedule table exists — the table is what\n    says a daemon is expected on this store; with no table this host never\n    ran one, which is a fact rather than an illness.\n    \"\"\"\n    import json\n    from datetime import datetime, timezone\n\n    from .gateway.daemon import DEFAULT_POLL_INTERVAL_SECONDS\n    from .gateway.scheduler import fold_schedules\n    from .gateway.server import HEARTBEAT_RELPATH\n\n    schedules = sorted(fold_schedules(tools_dir))\n    path = tools_dir.joinpath(*HEARTBEAT_RELPATH)\n    if not path.exists():\n        if schedules:\n            return DoctorCheck(\"gateway_heartbeat_fresh\", \"fail\", \"gateway_heartbeat_absent_with_schedules\", {\"schedules\": schedules})\n        return DoctorCheck(\"gateway_heartbeat_fresh\", \"ok\", \"gateway_not_deployed_here\", {\"schedules\": []})\n    try:\n        beat = json.loads(path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n        stamp = datetime.fromisoformat(str(beat.get(\"recorded_at\")).replace(\"Z\", \"+00:00\"))\n        interval = float(beat.get(\"poll_interval_seconds\") or DEFAULT_POLL_INTERVAL_SECONDS)\n    except (OSError, ValueError, TypeError, AttributeError):\n        status = \"fail\" if schedules else \"warn\"\n        return DoctorCheck(\"gateway_heartbeat_fresh\", status, \"gateway_heartbeat_unreadable\", {\"schedules\": schedules})\n    age = (datetime.now(timezone.utc) - stamp).total_seconds()\n    missed = age / interval if interval > 0 else float(\"inf\")\n    detail = {\"heartbeat_age_seconds\": int(age), \"poll_interval_seconds\": interval, \"missed_beats\": round(missed, 1),\n              \"stale_after_beats\": stale_after_beats, \"schedules\": schedules}\n    if missed > stale_after_beats:\n        return DoctorCheck(\"gateway_heartbeat_fresh\", \"fail\", f\"gateway_heartbeat_stale:{int(age)}s>{stale_after_beats}x{interval:g}s\", detail)\n    return DoctorCheck(\"gateway_heartbeat_fresh\", \"ok\", \"\", detail)\n\n\ndef _check_orchestrator(tools_dir: Path) -> DoctorCheck:\n    \"\"\"A streak of ``cycle_failed`` exits is a FAULT, not a series of bad\n    nights. The orchestrator fails closed on a failed cycle — nothing behind\n    it (planner dispatch, the convergence drainer) runs — so a store whose\n    last runs all died that way has not planned anything, whatever the other\n    organs say. Four such runs in a row on the live store (2026-08-21 →\n    2026-09-04) read as healthy here before this organ existed.\"\"\"\n    from .orchestrator_exit_history import read_orchestrator_exit_streak\n\n    streak = read_orchestrator_exit_streak(tools_dir)\n    detail = streak.to_dict()\n    if streak.all_failed:\n        return DoctorCheck(\n            \"orchestrator\", \"fail\",\n            f\"orchestrator_exits_all_cycle_failed:{len(streak.exits)}\", detail,\n        )\n    if streak.last_failed:\n        return DoctorCheck(\"orchestrator\", \"warn\", \"last_orchestrator_exit_cycle_failed\", detail)\n    if len(streak.exits) < 2:\n        return DoctorCheck(\"orchestrator\", \"ok\", \"insufficient_history\", detail)\n    return DoctorCheck(\"orchestrator\", \"ok\", \"\", detail)\n\n\ndef _check_tools(tools_dir: Path) -> DoctorCheck:\n    \"\"\"ARIA-HIGH-098 — the adapters that are not answering, by name and class.\n\n    A non-ok tool run no longer fails the night (``cycle_runtime_status``),\n    which is exactly why it needs an organ: a degraded tool used to announce\n    itself by killing the cycle, and now announces itself here. A streak that\n    reached ``TOOL_DEGRADATION_HUMAN_REQUIRED_STREAK`` is a FAIL (a record is\n    open for an operator); a shorter streak is a WARN naming the class and\n    the count. A QUARANTINED tool's streak counts the cycles it has sat out\n    (``tool_sit_out``: class ``quarantined``), so a tool ``tool_health``\n    benched reaches FAIL on the same third night as one that crashes —\n    quarantine is ``tool_health`` doing its job, but a tool out of the roster\n    until an operator un-quarantines it is not something a healthy readout\n    may be silent about; a quarantine with no cycle behind it yet is still\n    named (``tools_quarantined``).\"\"\"\n    from .tool_degradation import degradation_report\n\n    report = degradation_report(tools_dir)\n    detail = {\n        \"degraded\": report[\"degraded\"],\n        \"quarantined\": report[\"quarantined\"],\n        \"human_required_streak\": report[\"human_required_streak\"],\n    }\n    escalated = [entry[\"tool_id\"] for entry in report[\"degraded\"] if entry[\"human_required\"]]\n    if escalated:\n        return DoctorCheck(\"tools\", \"fail\", f\"tools_degraded_human_required:{','.join(escalated)}\", detail)\n    if report[\"degraded\"]:\n        named = \",\".join(\n            f\"{entry['tool_id']}={entry['degradation_class']}x{entry['consecutive_cycles']}\"\n            for entry in report[\"degraded\"]\n        )\n        return DoctorCheck(\"tools\", \"warn\", f\"tools_degraded:{named}\", detail)\n    if report[\"quarantined\"]:\n        return DoctorCheck(\"tools\", \"warn\", f\"tools_quarantined:{','.join(report['quarantined'])}\", detail)\n    return DoctorCheck(\"tools\", \"ok\", \"\", detail)\n\n\ndef _check_economy(tools_dir: Path) -> DoctorCheck:\n    \"\"\"Plan 032 Faz 032i — a standing effort downgrade is information; no accepted\n    result across a busy agent is a warning.\"\"\"\n    from .token_economy import read_recommendations, usage_per_accepted_result\n\n    stats = usage_per_accepted_result(base_dir=tools_dir)\n    downgrades = [f\"{r['target_agent']}/{r['role']}\" for r in read_recommendations(tools_dir) if r.get(\"kind\") == \"effort\" and r.get(\"action\") == \"downgrade\"]\n    starved = [f\"{s.target_agent}/{s.role}\" for s in stats if s.spawns >= 5 and s.accepted == 0]\n    detail = {\"stats\": [s.to_dict() for s in stats][:20], \"downgrades\": downgrades[-5:], \"starved\": starved}\n    if starved:\n        return DoctorCheck(\"economy\", \"warn\", f\"spawns_without_accepted_result:{len(starved)}\", detail)\n    return DoctorCheck(\"economy\", \"ok\", \"\" if stats else \"no_usage_rows\", detail)\n\n\nCALIBRATION_GATE_DOWNGRADE_WARN_DAYS: int = 7\n\n\ndef _check_calibration_gate(tools_dir: Path, *, now: Any | None = None) -> DoctorCheck:\n    \"\"\"Typed-judgment plan Phase 6 — an `enforce` the cycle keeps downgrading\n    is a configuration that has not taken effect: after seven days of\n    `configured=enforce, effective=measure_only` on the governance ledger the\n    operator hears it here, not from a ledger read.\"\"\"\n    from datetime import datetime, timedelta, timezone\n\n    from .ledger import load_jsonl\n\n    path = tools_dir / \"governance.jsonl\"\n    rows = [row for row in (load_jsonl(path) if path.exists() else []) if row.get(\"kind\") == \"calibration_gate\"]\n    if not rows:\n        return DoctorCheck(\"calibration_gate\", \"ok\", detail={\"rows\": 0})\n    latest = rows[-1].get(\"details\") or {}\n    detail = {\"configured\": latest.get(\"configured\"), \"effective\": latest.get(\"effective\"),\n              \"reason\": latest.get(\"reason\"), \"calibrated_judges\": latest.get(\"calibrated_judges\")}\n    if latest.get(\"configured\") != \"enforce\" or latest.get(\"effective\") != \"measure_only\":\n        return DoctorCheck(\"calibration_gate\", \"ok\", detail=detail)\n    # How long has the downgrade been continuous?\n    since = None\n    for row in reversed(rows):\n        entry = row.get(\"details\") or WARN\n561\t    when anything is due inside the window, or when a registry deadline has\n562\t    lapsed — the daily sweep plans that into BLOCKED through a sweep PR that\n563\t    lands when merged, so the doctor names it without declaring the store\n564\t    ill (132 such rows on the day this was written). OK otherwise. One reader\n565\t    (`deadlines.read_deadlines`) feeds this organ, the daily report and\n566\t    the `deadline_due` signal, so the three cannot name different dates.\"\"\"\n567\t    from .deadlines import name_rows, read_deadlines\n568\t\n569\t    readout = read_deadlines(tools_dir=tools_dir, workspace_root=workspace_root)\n570\t    detail = readout.to_dict()\n571\t    now = readout.now\n572\t    faults: list[str] = []\n573\t    if readout.undecided:\n574\t        faults.append(\"deadlines_undecided:\" + \",\".join(readout.undecided))\n575\t    if readout.lapsed_faults:\n576\t        faults.append(f\"deadlines_lapsed:{len(readout.lapsed_faults)}:\" + name_rows(readout.lapsed_faults, now))\n577\t    warnings: list[str] = []\n578\t    if readout.due_soon:\n579\t        warnings.append(f\"deadlines_due:{len(readout.due_soon)}:\" + name_rows(readout.due_soon, now))\n580\t    swept = tuple(row for row in readout.lapsed if not row.lapse_is_fault)\n581\t    if swept:\n582\t        warnings.append(f\"deadlines_lapsed_swept:{len(swept)}:\" + name_rows(swept, now))\n583\t    # The strongest verdict first; every part is named so the one-line\n584\t    # readout carries the whole picture and the detail carries every row.\n585\t    if faults:\n586\t        return DoctorCheck(\"deadlines\", \"fail\", \";\".join((*faults, *warnings)), detail)\n587\t    if warnings:\n588\t        return DoctorCheck(\"deadlines\", \"warn\", \";\".join(warnings), detail)\n589\t    return DoctorCheck(\"deadlines\", \"ok\", \"\", detail)\n590\t\n591\t\n592\tdef run_doctor(\n593\t    *,\n594\t    base_dir: str | Path | None = None,\n595\t    workspace_root: str | Path | None = None,\n596\t    claude_version_floor: str = CLAUDE_CLI_VERSION_FLOOR,\n597\t) -> DoctorReport:\n598\t    \"\"\"Run every organ probe and return the report. Read-only by construction:\n599\t    every probe is a reader, and the tools root is resolved read-only — an\n600\t    unbound root is itself a finding, never a directory the doctor creates.\"\"\"\n601\t    tools_dir = ensure_tools_dir_readonly(base_dir)\n602\t    workspace = Path(workspace_root).resolve() if workspace_root else Path.cwd().resolve()\n603\t    host_checks = (\n604\t        _guarded(\"providers\", _check_providers),\n605\t        _guarded(\"sandbox_backend\", _check_sandbox),\n606\t        _guarded(\"claude_cli\", lambda: _check_claude_cli(floor=claude_version_floor)),\n607\t        _guarded(\"habitat\", lambda: _check_habitat(workspace)),\n608\t    )\n609\t    if tools_dir is None:\n610\t        checks = (\n611\t            DoctorCheck(\n612\t                \"tools_root\", \"fail\", \"tools_root_unbound\",\n613\t                {\"base_dir\": str(base_dir) if base_dir else None},\n614\t            ),\n615\t            *host_checks,\n616\t        )\n617\t        return DoctorReport(checks=checks, tools_dir=\"\", workspace_root=str(workspace))\n618\t    store_checks = (\n619\t        _guarded(\"integrity\", lambda: _check_integrity(tools_dir, workspace)),\n620\t        _guarded(\"runtime_artifacts\", lambda: _check_runtime_artifacts(tools_dir, workspace)),\n621\t        _guarded(\"breakers\", lambda: _check_breakers(tools_dir)),\n622\t        _guarded(\"host_lease\", lambda: _check_host_lease(tools_dir)),\n623\t        _guarded(\"plan_ledger\", lambda: _check_plan_ledger(tools_dir)),\n624\t        _guarded(\"funnel\", lambda: _check_funnel(tools_dir)),\n625\t        _guarded(\"delivery_closure\", lambda: _check_delivery(tools_dir)),\n626\t        _guarded(\"queue\", lambda:            since = row.get(\"ts\") or row.get(\"recorded_at\")\n            continue\n        break\n    detail[\"downgraded_since\"] = since\n    try:\n        started = datetime.fromisoformat(str(since).replace(\"Z\", \"+00:00\")) if since else None\n    except ValueError:\n        started = None\n    current = now or datetime.now(timezone.utc)\n    if started is not None and current - started > timedelta(days=CALIBRATION_GATE_DOWNGRADE_WARN_DAYS):\n        return DoctorCheck(\"calibration_gate\", \"warn\", \"calibration_gate_enforce_downgraded_over_seven_days\", detail)\n    return DoctorCheck(\"calibration_gate\", \"ok\", detail=detail)\n\n\ndef _check_deadlines(tools_dir: Path, workspace_root: Path) -> DoctorCheck:\n    \"\"\"ARIA-MEDIUM-128 — a deadline the kernel will enforce is announced here\n    `DEADLINE_WARNING_DAYS` ahead, named, with days left.\n\n    FAIL when a source could not be read (`deadlines_undecided:<source>` —\n    an organ that cannot see a clock must not say the clock is fine) or when\n    a deadline whose lapse is a fault has lapsed (a waiver: the kernel lanes\n    are red; a HUMAN_REQUIRED SLA: the operator's promise is broken). WARN\n    when anything is due inside the window, or when a registry deadline has\n    lapsed — the daily sweep plans that into BLOCKED through a sweep PR that\n    lands when merged, so the doctor names it without declaring the store\n    ill (132 such rows on the day this was written). OK otherwise. One reader\n    (`deadlines.read_deadlines`) feeds this organ, the daily report and\n    the `deadline_due` signal, so the three cannot name different dates.\"\"\"\n    from .deadlines import name_rows, read_deadlines\n\n    readout = read_deadlines(tools_dir=tools_dir, workspace_root=workspace_root)\n    detail = readout.to_dict()\n    now = readout.now\n    faults: list[str] = []\n    if readout.undecided:\n        faults.append(\"deadlines_undecided:\" + \",\".join(readout.undecided))\n    if readout.lapsed_faults:\n        faults.append(f\"deadlines_lapsed:{len(readout.lapsed_faults)}:\" + name_rows(readout.lapsed_faults, now))\n    warnings: list[str] = []\n    if readout.due_soon:\n        warnings.append(f\"deadlines_due:{len(readout.due_soon)}:\" + name_rows(readout.due_soon, now))\n    swept = tuple(row for row in readout.lapsed if not row.lapse_is_fault)\n    if swept:\n        warnings.append(f\"deadlines_lapsed_swept:{len(swept)}:\" + name_rows(swept, now))\n    # The strongest verdict first; every part is named so the one-line\n    # readout carries the whole picture and the detail carries every row.\n    if faults:\n        return DoctorCheck(\"deadlines\", \"fail\", \";\".join((*faults, *warnings)), detail)\n    if warnings:\n        return DoctorCheck(\"deadlines\", \"warn\", \";\".join(warnings), detail)\n    return DoctorCheck(\"deadlines\", \"ok\", \"\", detail)\n\n\ndef run_doctor(\n    *,\n    base_dir: str | Path | None = None,\n    workspace_root: str | Path | None = None,\n    claude_version_floor: str = CLAUDE_CLI_VERSION_FLOOR,\n) -> DoctorReport:\n    \"\"\"Run every organ probe and return the report. Read-only by construction:\n    every probe is a reader, and the tools root is resolved read-only — an\n    unbound root is itself a finding, never a directory the doctor creates.\"\"\"\n    tools_dir = ensure_tools_dir_readonly(base_dir)\n    workspace = Path(workspace_root).resolve() if workspace_root else Path.cwd().resolve()\n    host_checks = (\n        _guarded(\"providers\", _check_providers),\n        _guarded(\"sandbox_backend\", _check_sandbox),\n        _guarded(\"claude_cli\", lambda: _check_claude_cli(floor=claude_version_floor)),\n        _guarded(\"habitat\", lambda: _check_habitat(workspace)),\n    )\n    if tools_dir is None:\n        checks = (\n            DoctorCheck(\n                \"tools_root\", \"fail\", \"tools_root_unbound\",\n                {\"base_dir\": str(base_dir) if base_dir else None},\n            ),\n            *host_checks,\n        )\n        return DoctorReport(checks=checks, tools_dir=\"\", workspace_root=str(workspace))\n    store_checks = (\n        _guarded(\"integrity\", lambda: _check_integrity(tools_dir, workspace)),\n        _guarded(\"runtime_artifacts\", lambda: _check_runtime_artifacts(tools_dir, workspace)),\n        _guarded(\"breakers\", lambda: _check_breakers(tools_dir)),\n        _guarded(\"host_lease\", lambda: _check_host_lease(tools_dir)),\n        _guarded(\"plan_ledger\", lambda: _check_plan_ledger(tools_dir)),\n        _guarded(\"funnel\", lambda: _check_funnel(tools_dir)),\n        _guarded(\"delivery_closure\", lambda: _check_delivery(tools_dir)),\n        _guarded(\"queue\", lambda: _check_queue(tools_dir)),\n        _guarded(\"control\", lambda: _check_control(tools_dir)),\n        _guarded(\"notifications\", lambda: _check_notifications(tools_dir)),\n        _guarded(\"gateway\", lambda: _check_gateway(tools_dir)),\n        _guarded(\"gateway_heartbeat_fresh\", lambda: _check_gateway_heartbeat_fresh(tools_dir)),\n        _guarded(\"economy\", lambda: _check_economy(tools_dir)),\n        _guarded(\"orchestrator\", lambda: _check_orchestrator(tools_dir)),\n        _guarded(\"tools\", lambda: _check_tools(tools_dir)),\n        _guarded(\"deadlines\", lambda: _check_deadlines(tools_dir, workspace)),\n        _guarded(\"calibration_gate\", lambda: _check_calibration_gate(tools_dir)),\n    )\n    return DoctorReport(\n        checks=(*store_checks, *host_checks),\n        tools_dir=str(tools_dir),\n        workspace_root=str(workspace),\n    )\n\n\ndef render_doctor_text(report: DoctorReport) -> str:\n    marks = {\"ok\": \"ok  \", \"warn\": \"WARN\", \"fail\": \"FAIL\"}\n    lines = [f\"aria doctor — {'healthy' if report.healthy else 'UNHEALTHY'} (exit {report.exit_code})\"]\n    for check in report.checks:\n        suffix = f\" — {check.reason}\" if check.reason else \"\"\n        lines.append(f\"  [{marks[check.status]}] {check.name}{suffix}\")\n    return \"\\n\".join(lines)\n\n\n__all__ = [\n    \"CLAUDE_CLI_VERSION_FLOOR\",\n    \"HEARTBEAT_STALE_AFTER_BEATS\",\n    \"DOCTOR_EXIT_HEALTHY\",\n    \"DOCTOR_EXIT_UNHEALTHY\",\n    \"DoctorCheck\",\n    \"DoctorReport\",\n    \"render_doctor_text\",\n    \"run_doctor\",\n]\n","numLines":125,"startLine":539,"totalLines":663}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01KHpw7vFHDYeEsH5SctoHwu","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# ARIA safety-control wiring ledger\n2\t\n3\t**What this file answers:** _is this ARIA safety control actually live, and who\n4\tchanged that?_\n5\t\n6\tIt exists because the failure mode this repository keeps rediscovering is not a\n7\tmissing control. It is a control that is written, unit-tested, exported, name-\n8\tpinned by an invariant — and called by nobody. `ORPHAN-CRITICAL-498`,\n9\t`ORPHAN-HIGH-455`, `ORPHAN-CRITICAL-420`, `ORPHAN-MEDIUM-571/572` and\n10\t`ORPHAN-MEDIUM-808` are all that one defect wearing different names. A green test\n11\tsuite is no evidence against it, because the tests call the control directly.\n12\t\n13\tWritten for a reader — human or model — who was not present when the change was\n14\tmade. **Do not trust this file; falsify it.** Every claim below has a command\n15\tnext to it.\n16\t\n17\t---\n18\t\n19\t## How to check the current state yourself\n20\t\n21\t```bash\n22\tcd aria-kernel\n23\tPYTHONPATH=.:.. python3 - <<'PY'\n24\tfrom pathlib import Path\n25\tfrom aria_kernel.control_reachability import declared_controls, unreachable_controls\n26\timport json, io\n27\troot = Path('..').resolve()\n28\td, u = declared_controls(root), unreachable_controls(root)\n29\tdorm = json.load(io.open('control-reachability.dormant.json', encoding='utf-8'))\n30\tprint('declared control verbs :', len(d))\n31\tprint('unreachable            :', len(u))\n32\tprint('waivers                :', len(dorm))\n33\tprint('unreachable, NO waiver :', sorted(set(u) - set(dorm)))   # must be []\n34\tprint('waived but reachable   :', sorted(set(dorm) - set(u)))   # must be []\n35\tPY\n36\t\n37\t# the gate itself, including the clock comparison on every waiver\n38\tPYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 PYTHONPATH=.:.. python3 -m unittest tests.test_control_reachability\n39\t```\n40\t\n41\tTo ask whether one specific control is wired, never grep — grep cannot tell a\n42\tcall from a mention, and this repo's comments name these controls constantly.\n43\tThat is how `ORPHAN-CRITICAL-428` was reported as wired when it was not:\n44\t\n45\t```bash\n46\tgrep -rn \"\\bmy_control(\" --include=*.py aria-kernel/aria_kernel tools | grep -v \"def my_control\"\n47\t```\n48\t\n49\t### Measurement on 2026-09-09\n50\t\n51\t|                               |     |\n52\t| ----------------------------- | --- |\n53\t| Declared control verbs        | 111 |\n54\t| Unreachable from production   | 14  |\n55\t| Covered by a dormancy waiver  | 14  |\n56\t| Unreachable with no waiver    | 0   |\n57\t| Waived but actually reachable | 0   |\n58\t\n59\tFor contrast, when `test_control_reachability.py` was written the measurement was\n60\t**18 of 85 (21%)**.\n61\t\n62\t---\n63\t\n64\t## The gate's known blind spot — read this before trusting a green run\n65\t\n66\t`control_reachability.CONTROL_VERBS` is exactly:\n67\t\n68\t```text\n69\tvalidate_  enforce_  assert_  require_  verify_  guard_  refuse_  check_\n70\t```\n71\t\n72\tA control whose name does not start with one of those prefixes **is invisible to\n73\tthe gate**. This is not hypothetical — it hid `ORPHAN-MEDIUM-808` for months:\n74\t\n75\t> A counter-based control has a completeness requirement — _increment, reset and\n76\t> decide are live together or not at all_ — that a name-prefix reachability scan\n77\t> cannot express. `oscillation_guard` had `guard_fix_dispatch` (visible, waived)\n78\t> and `record_resolution` (invisible, unwaived, and the one whose absence broke\n79\t> the contract).\n80\t\n81\t**Rule of thumb for a future reader:** when you find a dormant `guard_*` /\n82\t`assert_*`, do not stop at the gate's report. Look for its `record_*`, `emit_*`,\n83\t`_reset` and `_producer` counterparts and check those by hand. The gate reports\n84\tdeciders; it does not report the data they decide on.\n85\t\n86\t---\n87\t\n88\t## Standing rules for a dormant control\n89\t\n90\tThere are exactly **three** legitimate dispositions, and pushing `expires_on`\n91\tforward is not one of them. A waiver whose expiry nothing enforces is a waiver\n92\twith no expiry — which is the precise defect the clock comparison exists to catch\n93\t(`invariant-reachability.spec.ts` let twenty-five waivers sail a month past a\n94\tshared deadline in silence before it compared dates to the clock).\n95\t\n96\t1. **Delete it** — when a live path already answers the same question. This is\n97\t   the most common correct answer. The manifest states the hazard itself, on\n98\t   `assert_within_breaker`: _\"a second way to ask the same question is how two\n99\t   answers start to diverge.\"_\n100\t2. **Wire it** — when it guards a live path nothing else guards. Verify the whole\n101\t   contract first (see 808 below): wiring a decider onto an incomplete data path\n102\t   can be worse than dormancy.\n103\t3. **Keep it dormant with an honest waiver** — owner, reason, `expires_on`,\n104\t   `finding_id`. The reason must say what would make it wireable, not merely that\n105\t   it is not wired.\n106\t\n107\t---\n108\t\n109\t## 2026-09-09 — `ORPHAN-MEDIUM-808`, the oscillation guard\n110\t\n111\tCommit: `fix(aria): the oscillation guard counted forever and decided nothing`\n112\tAudit: `docs/reviews/zcode/2026-09-09-aria-autonomy-design-health.md`\n113\t\n114\t### The before-state, exactly\n115\t\n116\t`aria_kernel/oscillation_guard.py` (Plan 031 Gate B) exists to stop ARIA\n117\tping-ponging: fix X → X reopens → fix X again, forever, each fix correct in\n118\tisolation. It shipped a complete, unit-tested **three-part contract**. Production\n119\twired **one third** of it:\n120\t\n121\t```text\n122\trecord_reopen      (increment) : memory.py:1004                  ← LIVE\n123\trecord_resolution  (reset)     : tests only                      ← 0 production callers\n124\tguard_fix_dispatch (decide)    : tests only                      ← 0 production callers\n125\tassert_fix_dispatch_allowed    : tests only                      ← 0 production callers\n126\tis_oscillating     (predicate) : only from assert_fix_dispatch_… ← transitively dead\n127\t```\n128\t\n129\tThree comments in three modules described a consumer that did not exist:\n130\t\n131\t- `memory.py:1002` — _\"the fix dispatcher's `guard_fix_dispatch` decides\"_\n132\t  (there was no fix dispatcher, and nothing decided)\n133\t- `capability_gap.py:465` — _\"…for oscillation guard use. Nights are UTC dates,\n134\t  not rows…\"_ (shaping data for an absent reader)\n135\t- `observation_coverage.py:199` — _\"…the oscillation guard uses to tell a loop\n136\t  from a revision\"_\n137\t\n138\t**Why the reset was the half that mattered.** `reopen_streak` tail-scans\n139\tgovernance newest-first and stops at the first `finding_resolution_clean` for the\n140\tfingerprint. With no producer for that event, the streak was **monotonic** — it\n141\tcounted every reopen a belief had ever had, across the whole life of the\n142\trepository, and never subtracted. A belief that broke and healed three times over\n143\tthree months was indistinguishable from one ping-ponging inside one cycle.\n144\t\n145\tSo wiring the decider _first_ would have converted a dormant control into a\n146\t**permanent block**: the first belief to reach three lifetime reopens would be\n147\tescalated to `HUMAN_REQUIRED` and refused forever, with no path back. That is\n148\tstrictly worse than dormancy, and it is why the obvious move was the wrong one.\n149\t\n150\t### What was WIRED, and where\n151\t\n152\t**1. The reset — `memory.py`, in `_record_belief`, immediately after\n153\t`append_jsonl(... beliefs.jsonl ...)`:**\n154\t\n155\t```python\n156\tif int((existing or {}).get(\"needs_revalidation_cycles\", 0)) > 0 and not needs_revalidation_cycles:\n157\t    from .oscillation_guard import record_resolution\n158\t    record_resolution(fingerprint=f\"belief:{belief_id}\", cycle_id=cycle_id, base_dir=root)\n159\t```\n160\t\n161\tThe condition is a **state transition**, not a state: the belief _was_ in\n162\trevalidation, ARIA re-observed it, and its evidence holds again. Emitting on every\n163\tclean pass would silently disarm the guard; a test asserts that a still-broken\n164\tbelief re-observed does **not** reset (`test_a_belief_that_never_healed_keeps_its_streak`).\n165\t\n166\tThe fingerprint is spelled `belief:{belief_id}` — byte-identical to how the reopen\n167\tside spells it. One vocabulary, or the governance scan matches nothing and the\n168\tguard is decorative.\n169\t\n170\t**2. The decider — `promotion_controller.py`, inside\n171\t`promote_converged_plan_to_dispatch`, in the blocker-collection phase:**\n172\t\n173\t```python\n174\toscillation_fingerprint = _oscillation_fingerprint(paths, pressure_event_id)\n175\tif oscillation_fingerprint is not None:\n176\t    try:\n177\t        guard_fix_dispatch(fingerprint=..., cycle_id=cycle_id, base_dir=root, context={...})\n178\t    except GovernanceError:\n179\t        blockers.append(\"oscillating_fingerprint\")\n180\t```\n181\t\n182\tWhy _that_ function: it is the single throat where a CONVERGED plan becomes a\n183\tmaterialized `aria/dispatch-request/v2` row a worker acts on. It is reachable\n184\ttoday from `cli.py:4441` — the waiver's _\"wire it when that lane goes live\"_ was\n185\talready satisfied and nobody had rechecked.\n186\t\n187\tWhy the raise is caught: the throat's contract is to report **every** reason a\n188\tpromotion was refused. A plan blocked for four reasons must name four, or the\n189\toperator fixes one and returns to find the next. The escalation still happens\n190\texactly once, inside the module that owns the threshold — there is no second copy\n191\tof the rule at the callsite.\n192\t\n193\t**3. The fingerprint lookup — new helper `_oscillation_fingerprint`:**\n194\t\n195\tResolves `pressure_event_id → belief_id` by reading the **raw** pressure ledger\n196\t(`paths.ledgers[\"pressure\"]`), deliberately **not**\n197\t`effective_workspace_pressures`. The effective view filters by decay, and a\n198\tpressure that has decayed out is still the pressure the plan was written for —\n199\tasking the decayed view would skip the guard for exactly the slow, long-running\n200\tping-pong it exists to catch.\n201\t\n202\tReturns `None` for a promotion with no pressure (an operator promotion has no\n203\treopen history to consult) and for a pressure with no `belief_id` (the counter's\n204\tkey space holds only `belief:<id>` today, so a tool-derived pressure has nothing\n205\tto look up). Both are _the absence of a question_, not permission to skip an\n206\tanswer — and a test pins that an unrelated belief's history cannot leak in.\n207\t\n208\t### What was DELETED, and what it was\n209\t\n210\tTwo non-escalating siblings, removed rather than given callers. Both asked the\n211\tdecider's question without performing its refusal — the \"second answer\" the\n212\tmanifest itself warns about.\n213\t\n214\t**`is_oscillating`** — read-only predicate, transitively dead (its only caller was\n215\tthe function below):\n216\t\n217\t```python\n218\tdef is_oscillating(*, fingerprint: str, base_dir: Any = None,\n219\t                   threshold: int = DEFAULT_OSCILLATION_THRESHOLD) -> bool:\n220\t    \"\"\"Read-only: True iff the reopen streak has reached the threshold.\"\"\"\n221\t    if threshold < 1:\n222\t        raise GovernanceError(\"threshold must be >= 1\")\n223\t    return reopen_streak(fingerprint=fingerprint, base_dir=base_dir) >= threshold\n224\t```\n225\t\n226\t**`assert_fix_dispatch_allowed`** — raised without escalating:\n227\t\n228\t```python\n229\tdef assert_fix_dispatch_allowed(*, fingerprint: str, base_dir: Any = None,\n230\t                                threshold: int = DEFAULT_OSCILLATION_THRESHOLD) -> None:\n231\t    \"\"\"Raise if ``fingerprint`` is oscillation-blocked (read-only).\n232\t\n233\t    The cheap pre-dispatch guard. Does NOT escalate (no HUMAN_REQUIRED write) —\n234\t    use ``guard_fix_dispatch`` for the escalate-and-block decision.\n235\t    \"\"\"\n236\t    if is_oscillating(fingerprint=fingerprint, base_dir=base_dir, threshold=threshold):\n237\t        raise GovernanceError(\n238\t            f\"oscillation_fix_dispatch_blocked: fingerprint={fingerprint!r} has \"\n239\t            f\"reopened >= {threshold} times without a clean resolution; \"\n240\t            f\"autonomous fix dispatch refused — operator must intervene\"\n241\t        )\n242\t```\n243\t\n244\t**If you are about to restore either of them: don't, unless you have a caller that\n245\tneeds a check WITHOUT an escalation, and you can say why that caller must not\n246\tescalate.** Its waiver had argued the pair must be _\"wired or neither\"_ to avoid\n247\t\"the appearance of oscillation control without the refusal\". The refusal now\n248\texists in `guard_fix_dispatch`; a second, quieter answer beside it is the drift\n249\thazard, not a safety net. Their tests were removed with them; the surviving\n250\tbehaviour is covered by `guard_fix_dispatch`'s own tests.\n251\t\n252\tAlso removed: their two entries from `control-reachability.dormant.json`\n253\t(expired waivers 10 → 8). The manifest raised no stale-waiver complaint, which is\n254\tindependent confirmation that `guard_fix_dispatch` is genuinely reachable now.\n255\t\n256\t### What was deliberately NOT done\n257\t\n258\t- **The comments in `capability_gap.py:465` and `observation_coverage.py:199`\n259\t  were left as-is** — they became TRUE with this change (there is now an active\n260\t  consumer), so editing them would have been churn.\n261\t- **No change to the threshold** (`DEFAULT_OSCILLATION_THRESHOLD = 3`) or to\n262\t  `record_reopen`'s call site. The reopen half was never wrong.\n263\t- **No `finding:<sha>` producer was added.** The counter's key space documents\n264\t  both `finding:<sha>` and `belief:<id>`; only `belief:` is ever produced. The\n265\t  guard therefore fires only for belief-derived pressures today. That is the\n266\t  current shape of the system, recorded here so the next reader does not mistake\n267\t  it for coverage.\n268\t\n269\t### Verification performed\n270\t\n271\t- 7 new tests across `tests/test_oscillation_resolution_wiring.py` and\n272\t  `tests/test_oscillation_promotion_gate.py`.\n273\t- **Negative controls run for both halves** — with the fix reverted, 2 of 3 and 2\n274\t  of 4 fail, naming the real defect: _\"3 separate break/heal rounds accumulated\n275\t  into a loop verdict\"_.\n276\t- 131 tests green across memory, promotion, mission, dispatch and the guard.\n277\t\n278\t---\n279\t\n280\t## 2026-09-09 — `ORPHAN-HIGH-573`, the real-mode environment guard\n281\t\n282\tCommit: `fix(aria): real-mode eval ran without its own environment precondition`\n283\t\n284\t### Before\n285\t\n286\t`artifact_safety.assert_real_mode_env_safe(env)` refuses a real-mode run when any\n287\tname in `FORBIDDEN_REAL_MODE_ENV` (today: `CODEX_OSS_DEBUG`) is set to `\"1\"`. It\n288\thad **zero callers anywhere** — only its definition and its `__all__` entry.\n289\t\n290\tIts waiver carried a `CORRECTED 2026-08-06` note, which re-reading the CLI\n291\tconfirmed is still accurate: the _original_ waiver claimed the mode is\n292\tunreachable because `run_agent_eval` defaults to `mock_mode=True`. False.\n293\t`eval-run --no-mock-mode` is registered (`cli.py:1105`), needs only\n294\t`--real-envelope-file` beside it (`cli.py:1103`), and `cli.py:3719` computes\n295\t`mock_mode = not args.no_mock_mode`. An unguarded **live** path, not a dormant\n296\tfuture one — bounded because the flag is operator-typed rather than scheduled,\n297\twhich is why it stayed MEDIUM.\n298\t\n299\t### Wired\n300\t\n301\t`agent_eval.run_agent_eval`, as the **first** statement of the `else:` (real-mode)\n302\tbranch, ahead of the provenance preconditions:\n303\t\n304\t```python\n305\tassert_real_mode_env_safe(dict(os.environ))\n306\t```\n307\t\n308\tOrder is the substance, not the placement detail. Without it the first refusal a\n309\tcaller sees is `mock_mode=False requires real_response_envelope` — proven by the\n310\tnegative control, which produced exactly that error — meaning the run has already\n311\tbegun reading ledgers and binding an invocation under the debugger environment\n312\tthe guard exists to keep it out of.\n313\t\n314\tNote that `enforce_profile_for_write` and `_read_fixture` still run before the\n315\tbranch. That is correct: reading a fixture is not the hazard; _running real mode_\n316\tis.\n317\t\n318\t### Verification\n319\t\n320\t4 tests in `tests/test_real_mode_env_guard.py`, including one pinning the\n321\tordering and one pinning the blast radius (mock mode completes normally with the\n322\tvariable set, so wiring this cannot break a scheduled lane). Negative control\n323\trun. 41 tests green across the eval modules. Waiver removed; expired waivers\n324\t10 → 7 cumulative with the oscillation work.\n325\t\n326\t## 2026-09-09 — `ORPHAN-HIGH-573`, collapsing the duplicate entry points\n327\t\n328\tCommit: `refactor(aria): four ways to ask a question the live path already answers`\n329\t\n330\tEvery deletion below was preceded by PROVING the live answer exists. Two of the\n331\tfive candidates did not survive that proof and were NOT deleted — which is the\n332\tpoint of doing it in that order.\n333\t\n334\t### Deleted (proof in hand)\n335\t\n336\t| Control | The live answer, verified |\n337\t| ------- | ------------------------- |\n338\t\n339\tTheir tests were repointed at the surviving path rather than dropped:\n340\tthe breaker test now asserts on `evaluate_breaker`'s verdict directly, and the\n341\tdisjointness tests build the three dispatch rows themselves. Coverage is\n342\tunchanged; only the second spelling is gone.\n343\t\n344\t### Renamed, not deleted\n345\t\n346\t`check_remaining_budget` → **`remaining_reservation_budget`** (`budget.py`).\n347\t\n348\tReading it proved the waiver wrong: it returns `reserved - reconciled` as a\n349\tfloat and refuses nothing, so it is an **accessor**, not the duplicate of\n350\t`assert_within_budget` (which raises) that the waiver described. Its only\n351\tconsumer is `test_phase_v8_0_prerequisites`, observing reserve/reconcile\n352\tarithmetic — real coverage that deleting would have destroyed.\n353\t\n354\tThe defect was the NAME: `check_` is one of the eight `CONTROL_VERBS` prefixes,\n355\tso the gate demanded a production caller for something that is not a control.\n356\tRenaming fixes the classification at the source instead of carrying a waiver that\n357\tsays something untrue about the code.\n358\t\n359\t### Kept, with the reason replaced because the old one was FALSE\n360\t\n361\t- **`verify_workflow_registry`** — waiver claimed \"the equivalent assertion runs\n362\t  in CI as a TypeScript invariant\". Checked: no spec under `tests/invariants`\n363\t  references `WORKFLOW_CONTRACTS`, and the nearest one asserts something else\n364\t  (that a job running kernel code provisions the kernel first). The guarantee is\n365\t  **unduplicated**: preflight's live `verify_workflow_contract` validates ONE\n366\t  contract and by construction only visits workflows that already have one, so an\n367\t  ARIA workflow added with neither a contract nor an audited exclusion is\n368\t  invisible to it. Deleting would have removed a real control on a false premise.\n369\t\n370\t  Wiring it into `verify_workflow_preflight` was **attempted and reverted**: that\n371\t  preflight legitimately runs against synthetic workspaces where the workflow\n372\t  YAMLs are absent, so the inventory verdict is invalid there for a reason that is\n373\t  not a defect (`test_workflow_enterprise_preflight` proved it immediately). A\n374\t  repo-wide invariant needs a caller that always sees the real repo. That entry\n375\t  point — a CLI verb plus a CI step — is the open decision.\n376\t\n377\t- **`validate_file`** — waiver read as dead code (\"no CLI verb attached\"). It is\n378\t  the ENGINE of the V4 narrative-shape invariant\n379\t  (`tests/invariants/v4/test_phase_v4_b_narrative_shape.py:62,66`), which runs it\n380\t  over every agent file. Its consumer is an invariant test, which is this\n381\t  control's intended and sufficient consumer.\n382\t\n383\t- **`validate_request`** — WIRED (ARIA-HIGH-104, 2026-09-12; the waiver is gone).\n384\t  The vocabularies are reconciled: the queue row `create_agent_invocation_request`\n385\t  appends IS the `aria/agent-request/v1` envelope (`$schema` written from\n386\t  `agent_contract.REQUEST_SCHEMA`; `forbidden_scope` and `validation_commands`\n387\t  always written), and for every role in `agent_contract.CONTRACT_ENFORCED_ROLES`\n388\t  the queue calls `validate_request(row, base_dir)` before the append. The set's\n389\t  boundary is what the contract binds — a request to its cycle (`cycle_id`) and to\n390\t  the plan revision it works from (`convergence_id` + `plan_revision_hash`); the\n391\t  implementation role has both on every production mint. The operator-driven\n392\t  judge, curation, questioning and self-change lanes run in no cycle and implement\n393\t  no plan revision, so the contract has nothing to bind them to; their rows are\n394\t  held by the queue's field validation at mint and `validate_response` at submit.\n395\t  See CONTRACTS §12.17.\n396\t\n397\t### The classification problem, now seen three ways\n398\t\n399\t`CONTROL_VERBS` decides what the gate can see, and it is wrong in both\n400\tdirections:\n401\t\n402\t|                     |                                                                     |\n403\t| ------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n404\t| `record_resolution` | a real control the prefix list **cannot see** (`ORPHAN-MEDIUM-808`) |\n405\t\n406\tThat rule is right for a control meant to run in production and wrong for one\n407\twhose consumer is the invariant itself. Teaching the gate that category is open\n408\twork; it is named here so the next reader does not rediscover it a fourth time.\n409\t\n410\t### Result\n411\t\n412\tExpired dormancy waivers **10 → 0**. `test_control_reachability` passes 8/8, and\n413\twith it the `aria-kernel` / `unittest` check that had been red on `main` since\n414\t2026-09-07. 126 tests green across every module touched.\n415\t\n416\t## Open items a future reader should not re-derive\n417\t\n418\tMeasured 2026-09-09; check them before acting, they may have moved.\n419\t\n420\t| Control             | Disposition    | Why                                                                                        |\n421\t| ------------------- | -------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |\n422\t| `verify_branch_tip` | keep (in date) | `merge_pr_if_ready` does its own inline head-SHA comparison; PLAN Wave 8 collapses the two |\n423\t\n424\tWhat 2026-09-09 actually taught, now that the deletions have been done rather\n425\tthan planned:\n426\t\n427\t**Five controls were nominated for deletion on the strength of their waivers.\n428\tThree survived the proof and were deleted. Two did not** — one guarded something\n429\tunduplicated (`verify_workflow_registry`) and one was not a control at all\n430\t(`check_remaining_budget`). A sixth (`validate_file`) had a waiver that read as\n431\tdead code and turned out to be an invariant's engine.\n432\t\n433\tSo the instinct \"at this stage the healthy move is usually removal\" is right\n434\tabout the population and useless as a rule, because it is wrong about a third of\n435\tthe individuals. The rule that survives is the ORDER:\n436\t\n437\t> Prove the live answer FIRST, by reading it, and only then delete. A waiver's\n438\t> stated reason is a lead, not evidence — three of the ones checked today were\n439\t> materially false, and each would have removed or hidden a real control if\n440\t> followed.\n441\t\n442\tDeleting a proven duplicate reduces the surface the safety system has to keep\n443\tconsistent with itself; it does not weaken the system and it is not \"silencing\"\n444\tthe gate — the gate stays armed, with less to be wrong about. Deleting an\n445\tUNPROVEN one is how a repository loses a control and keeps the green check.\n446\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"15e2d44a-5660-4a51-a46d-eca1518eadb6","uuid":"da3dd01b-3b29-4866-aa27-f09364e17cc6","timestamp":"2026-09-25T10:47:54.639Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-consensus-arbiter-00bb0e8ea72e/docs/aria/SAFETY-CONTROL-WIRING-LEDGER.md","content":"# ARIA safety-control wiring ledger\n\n**What this file answers:** _is this ARIA safety control actually live, and who\nchanged that?_\n\nIt exists because the failure mode this repository keeps rediscovering is not a\nmissing control. It is a control that is written, unit-tested, exported, name-\npinned by an invariant — and called by nobody. `ORPHAN-CRITICAL-498`,\n`ORPHAN-HIGH-455`, `ORPHAN-CRITICAL-420`, `ORPHAN-MEDIUM-571/572` and\n`ORPHAN-MEDIUM-808` are all that one defect wearing different names. A green test\nsuite is no evidence against it, because the tests call the control directly.\n\nWritten for a reader — human or model — who was not present when the change was\nmade. **Do not trust this file; falsify it.** Every claim below has a command\nnext to it.\n\n---\n\n## How to check the current state yourself\n\n```bash\ncd aria-kernel\nPYTHONPATH=.:.. python3 - <<'PY'\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom aria_kernel.control_reachability import declared_controls, unreachable_controls\nimport json, io\nroot = Path('..').resolve()\nd, u = declared_controls(root), unreachable_controls(root)\ndorm = json.load(io.open('control-reachability.dormant.json', encoding='utf-8'))\nprint('declared control verbs :', len(d))\nprint('unreachable            :', len(u))\nprint('waivers                :', len(dorm))\nprint('unreachable, NO waiver :', sorted(set(u) - set(dorm)))   # must be []\nprint('waived but reachable   :', sorted(set(dorm) - set(u)))   # must be []\nPY\n\n# the gate itself, including the clock comparison on every waiver\nPYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 PYTHONPATH=.:.. python3 -m unittest tests.test_control_reachability\n```\n\nTo ask whether one specific control is wired, never grep — grep cannot tell a\ncall from a mention, and this repo's comments name these controls constantly.\nThat is how `ORPHAN-CRITICAL-428` was reported as wired when it was not:\n\n```bash\ngrep -rn \"\\bmy_control(\" --include=*.py aria-kernel/aria_kernel tools | grep -v \"def my_control\"\n```\n\n### Measurement on 2026-09-09\n\n|                               |     |\n| ----------------------------- | --- |\n| Declared control verbs        | 111 |\n| Unreachable from production   | 14  |\n| Covered by a dormancy waiver  | 14  |\n| Unreachable with no waiver    | 0   |\n| Waived but actually reachable | 0   |\n\nFor contrast, when `test_control_reachability.py` was written the measurement was\n**18 of 85 (21%)**.\n\n---\n\n## The gate's known blind spot — read this before trusting a green run\n\n`control_reachability.CONTROL_VERBS` is exactly:\n\n```text\nvalidate_  enforce_  assert_  require_  verify_  guard_  refuse_  check_\n```\n\nA control whose name does not start with one of those prefixes **is invisible to\nthe gate**. This is not hypothetical — it hid `ORPHAN-MEDIUM-808` for months:\n\n> A counter-based control has a completeness requirement — _increment, reset and\n> decide are live together or not at all_ — that a name-prefix reachability scan\n> cannot express. `oscillation_guard` had `guard_fix_dispatch` (visible, waived)\n> and `record_resolution` (invisible, unwaived, and the one whose absence broke\n> the contract).\n\n**Rule of thumb for a future reader:** when you find a dormant `guard_*` /\n`assert_*`, do not stop at the gate's report. Look for its `record_*`, `emit_*`,\n`_reset` and `_producer` counterparts and check those by hand. The gate reports\ndeciders; it does not report the data they decide on.\n\n---\n\n## Standing rules for a dormant control\n\nThere are exactly **three** legitimate dispositions, and pushing `expires_on`\nforward is not one of them. A waiver whose expiry nothing enforces is a waiver\nwith no expiry — which is the precise defect the clock comparison exists to catch\n(`invariant-reachability.spec.ts` let twenty-five waivers sail a month past a\nshared deadline in silence before it compared dates to the clock).\n\n1. **Delete it** — when a live path already answers the same question. This is\n   the most common correct answer. The manifest states the hazard itself, on\n   `assert_within_breaker`: _\"a second way to ask the same question is how two\n   answers start to diverge.\"_\n2. **Wire it** — when it guards a live path nothing else guards. Verify the whole\n   contract first (see 808 below): wiring a decider onto an incomplete data path\n   can be worse than dormancy.\n3. **Keep it dormant with an honest waiver** — owner, reason, `expires_on`,\n   `finding_id`. The reason must say what would make it wireable, not merely that\n   it is not wired.\n\n---\n\n## 2026-09-09 — `ORPHAN-MEDIUM-808`, the oscillation guard\n\nCommit: `fix(aria): the oscillation guard counted forever and decided nothing`\nAudit: `docs/reviews/zcode/2026-09-09-aria-autonomy-design-health.md`\n\n### The before-state, exactly\n\n`aria_kernel/oscillation_guard.py` (Plan 031 Gate B) exists to stop ARIA\nping-ponging: fix X → X reopens → fix X again, forever, each fix correct in\nisolation. It shipped a complete, unit-tested **three-part contract**. Production\nwired **one third** of it:\n\n```text\nrecord_reopen      (increment) : memory.py:1004                  ← LIVE\nrecord_resolution  (reset)     : tests only                      ← 0 production callers\nguard_fix_dispatch (decide)    : tests only                      ← 0 production callers\nassert_fix_dispatch_allowed    : tests only                      ← 0 production callers\nis_oscillating     (predicate) : only from assert_fix_dispatch_… ← transitively dead\n```\n\nThree comments in three modules described a consumer that did not exist:\n\n- `memory.py:1002` — _\"the fix dispatcher's `guard_fix_dispatch` decides\"_\n  (there was no fix dispatcher, and nothing decided)\n- `capability_gap.py:465` — _\"…for oscillation guard use. Nights are UTC dates,\n  not rows…\"_ (shaping data for an absent reader)\n- `observation_coverage.py:199` — _\"…the oscillation guard uses to tell a loop\n  from a revision\"_\n\n**Why the reset was the half that mattered.** `reopen_streak` tail-scans\ngovernance newest-first and stops at the first `finding_resolution_clean` for the\nfingerprint. With no producer for that event, the streak was **monotonic** — it\ncounted every reopen a belief had ever had, across the whole life of the\nrepository, and never subtracted. A belief that broke and healed three times over\nthree months was indistinguishable from one ping-ponging inside one cycle.\n\nSo wiring the decider _first_ would have converted a dormant control into a\n**permanent block**: the first belief to reach three lifetime reopens would be\nescalated to `HUMAN_REQUIRED` and refused forever, with no path back. That is\nstrictly worse than dormancy, and it is why the obvious move was the wrong one.\n\n### What was WIRED, and where\n\n**1. The reset — `memory.py`, in `_record_belief`, immediately after\n`append_jsonl(... beliefs.jsonl ...)`:**\n\n```python\nif int((existing or {}).get(\"needs_revalidation_cycles\", 0)) > 0 and not needs_revalidation_cycles:\n    from .oscillation_guard import record_resolution\n    record_resolution(fingerprint=f\"belief:{belief_id}\", cycle_id=cycle_id, base_dir=root)\n```\n\nThe condition is a **state transition**, not a state: the belief _was_ in\nrevalidation, ARIA re-observed it, and its evidence holds again. Emitting on every\nclean pass would silently disarm the guard; a test asserts that a still-broken\nbelief re-observed does **not** reset (`test_a_belief_that_never_healed_keeps_its_streak`).\n\nThe fingerprint is spelled `belief:{belief_id}` — byte-identical to how the reopen\nside spells it. One vocabulary, or the governance scan matches nothing and the\nguard is decorative.\n\n**2. The decider — `promotion_controller.py`, inside\n`promote_converged_plan_to_dispatch`, in the blocker-collection phase:**\n\n```python\noscillation_fingerprint = _oscillation_fingerprint(paths, pressure_event_id)\nif oscillation_fingerprint is not None:\n    try:\n        guard_fix_dispatch(fingerprint=..., cycle_id=cycle_id, base_dir=root, context={...})\n    except GovernanceError:\n        blockers.append(\"oscillating_fingerprint\")\n```\n\nWhy _that_ function: it is the single throat where a CONVERGED plan becomes a\nmaterialized `aria/dispatch-request/v2` row a worker acts on. It is reachable\ntoday from `cli.py:4441` — the waiver's _\"wire it when that lane goes live\"_ was\nalready satisfied and nobody had rechecked.\n\nWhy the raise is caught: the throat's contract is to report **every** reason a\npromotion was refused. A plan blocked for four reasons must name four, or the\noperator fixes one and returns to find the next. The escalation still happens\nexactly once, inside the module that owns the threshold — there is no second copy\nof the rule at the callsite.\n\n**3. The fingerprint lookup — new helper `_oscillation_fingerprint`:**\n\nResolves `pressure_event_id → belief_id` by reading the **raw** pressure ledger\n(`paths.ledgers[\"pressure\"]`), deliberately **not**\n`effective_workspace_pressures`. The effective view filters by decay, and a\npressure that has decayed out is still the pressure the plan was written for —\nasking the decayed view would skip the guard for exactly the slow, long-running\nping-pong it exists to catch.\n\nReturns `None` for a promotion with no pressure (an operator promotion has no\nreopen history to consult) and for a pressure with no `belief_id` (the counter's\nkey space holds only `belief:<id>` today, so a tool-derived pressure has nothing\nto look up). Both are _the absence of a question_, not permission to skip an\nanswer — and a test pins that an unrelated belief's history cannot leak in.\n\n### What was DELETED, and what it was\n\nTwo non-escalating siblings, removed rather than given callers. Both asked the\ndecider's question without performing its refusal — the \"second answer\" the\nmanifest itself warns about.\n\n**`is_oscillating`** — read-only predicate, transitively dead (its only caller was\nthe function below):\n\n```python\ndef is_oscillating(*, fingerprint: str, base_dir: Any = None,\n                   threshold: int = DEFAULT_OSCILLATION_THRESHOLD) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"Read-only: True iff the reopen streak has reached the threshold.\"\"\"\n    if threshold < 1:\n        raise GovernanceError(\"threshold must be >= 1\")\n    return reopen_streak(fingerprint=fingerprint, base_dir=base_dir) >= threshold\n```\n\n**`assert_fix_dispatch_allowed`** — raised without escalating:\n\n```python\ndef assert_fix_dispatch_allowed(*, fingerprint: str, base_dir: Any = None,\n                                threshold: int = DEFAULT_OSCILLATION_THRESHOLD) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Raise if ``fingerprint`` is oscillation-blocked (read-only).\n\n    The cheap pre-dispatch guard. Does NOT escalate (no HUMAN_REQUIRED write) —\n    use ``guard_fix_dispatch`` for the escalate-and-block decision.\n    \"\"\"\n    if is_oscillating(fingerprint=fingerprint, base_dir=base_dir, threshold=threshold):\n        raise GovernanceError(\n            f\"oscillation_fix_dispatch_blocked: fingerprint={fingerprint!r} has \"\n            f\"reopened >= {threshold} times without a clean resolution; \"\n            f\"autonomous fix dispatch refused — operator must intervene\"\n        )\n```\n\n**If you are about to restore either of them: don't, unless you have a caller that\nneeds a check WITHOUT an escalation, and you can say why that caller must not\nescalate.** Its waiver had argued the pair must be _\"wired or neither\"_ to avoid\n\"the appearance of oscillation control without the refusal\". The refusal now\nexists in `guard_fix_dispatch`; a second, quieter answer beside it is the drift\nhazard, not a safety net. Their tests were removed with them; the surviving\nbehaviour is covered by `guard_fix_dispatch`'s own tests.\n\nAlso removed: their two entries from `control-reachability.dormant.json`\n(expired waivers 10 → 8). The manifest raised no stale-waiver complaint, which is\nindependent confirmation that `guard_fix_dispatch` is genuinely reachable now.\n\n### What was deliberately NOT done\n\n- **The comments in `capability_gap.py:465` and `observation_coverage.py:199`\n  were left as-is** — they became TRUE with this change (there is now an active\n  consumer), so editing them would have been churn.\n- **No change to the threshold** (`DEFAULT_OSCILLATION_THRESHOLD = 3`) or to\n  `record_reopen`'s call site. The reopen half was never wrong.\n- **No `finding:<sha>` producer was added.** The counter's key space documents\n  both `finding:<sha>` and `belief:<id>`; only `belief:` is ever produced. The\n  guard therefore fires only for belief-derived pressures today. That is the\n  current shape of the system, recorded here so the next reader does not mistake\n  it for coverage.\n\n### Verification performed\n\n- 7 new tests across `tests/test_oscillation_resolution_wiring.py` and\n  `tests/test_oscillation_promotion_gate.py`.\n- **Negative controls run for both halves** — with the fix reverted, 2 of 3 and 2\n  of 4 fail, naming the real defect: _\"3 separate break/heal rounds accumulated\n  into a loop verdict\"_.\n- 131 tests green across memory, promotion, mission, dispatch and the guard.\n\n---\n\n## 2026-09-09 — `ORPHAN-HIGH-573`, the real-mode environment guard\n\nCommit: `fix(aria): real-mode eval ran without its own environment precondition`\n\n### Before\n\n`artifact_safety.assert_real_mode_env_safe(env)` refuses a real-mode run when any\nname in `FORBIDDEN_REAL_MODE_ENV` (today: `CODEX_OSS_DEBUG`) is set to `\"1\"`. It\nhad **zero callers anywhere** — only its definition and its `__all__` entry.\n\nIts waiver carried a `CORRECTED 2026-08-06` note, which re-reading the CLI\nconfirmed is still accurate: the _original_ waiver claimed the mode is\nunreachable because `run_agent_eval` defaults to `mock_mode=True`. False.\n`eval-run --no-mock-mode` is registered (`cli.py:1105`), needs only\n`--real-envelope-file` beside it (`cli.py:1103`), and `cli.py:3719` computes\n`mock_mode = not args.no_mock_mode`. An unguarded **live** path, not a dormant\nfuture one — bounded because the flag is operator-typed rather than scheduled,\nwhich is why it stayed MEDIUM.\n\n### Wired\n\n`agent_eval.run_agent_eval`, as the **first** statement of the `else:` (real-mode)\nbranch, ahead of the provenance preconditions:\n\n```python\nassert_real_mode_env_safe(dict(os.environ))\n```\n\nOrder is the substance, not the placement detail. Without it the first refusal a\ncaller sees is `mock_mode=False requires real_response_envelope` — proven by the\nnegative control, which produced exactly that error — meaning the run has already\nbegun reading ledgers and binding an invocation under the debugger environment\nthe guard exists to keep it out of.\n\nNote that `enforce_profile_for_write` and `_read_fixture` still run before the\nbranch. That is correct: reading a fixture is not the hazard; _running real mode_\nis.\n\n### Verification\n\n4 tests in `tests/test_real_mode_env_guard.py`, including one pinning the\nordering and one pinning the blast radius (mock mode completes normally with the\nvariable set, so wiring this cannot break a scheduled lane). Negative control\nrun. 41 tests green across the eval modules. Waiver removed; expired waivers\n10 → 7 cumulative with the oscillation work.\n\n## 2026-09-09 — `ORPHAN-HIGH-573`, collapsing the duplicate entry points\n\nCommit: `refactor(aria): four ways to ask a question the live path already answers`\n\nEvery deletion below was preceded by PROVING the live answer exists. Two of the\nfive candidates did not survive that proof and were NOT deleted — which is the\npoint of doing it in that order.\n\n### Deleted (proof in hand)\n\n| Control | The live answer, verified |\n| ------- | ------------------------- |\n\nTheir tests were repointed at the surviving path rather than dropped:\nthe breaker test now asserts on `evaluate_breaker`'s verdict directly, and the\ndisjointness tests build the three dispatch rows themselves. Coverage is\nunchanged; only the second spelling is gone.\n\n### Renamed, not deleted\n\n`check_remaining_budget` → **`remaining_reservation_budget`** (`budget.py`).\n\nReading it proved the waiver wrong: it returns `reserved - reconciled` as a\nfloat and refuses nothing, so it is an **accessor**, not the duplicate of\n`assert_within_budget` (which raises) that the waiver described. Its only\nconsumer is `test_phase_v8_0_prerequisites`, observing reserve/reconcile\narithmetic — real coverage that deleting would have destroyed.\n\nThe defect was the NAME: `check_` is one of the eight `CONTROL_VERBS` prefixes,\nso the gate demanded a production caller for something that is not a control.\nRenaming fixes the classification at the source instead of carrying a waiver that\nsays something untrue about the code.\n\n### Kept, with the reason replaced because the old one was FALSE\n\n- **`verify_workflow_registry`** — waiver claimed \"the equivalent assertion runs\n  in CI as a TypeScript invariant\". Checked: no spec under `tests/invariants`\n  references `WORKFLOW_CONTRACTS`, and the nearest one asserts something else\n  (that a job running kernel code provisions the kernel first). The guarantee is\n  **unduplicated**: preflight's live `verify_workflow_contract` validates ONE\n  contract and by construction only visits workflows that already have one, so an\n  ARIA workflow added with neither a contract nor an audited exclusion is\n  invisible to it. Deleting would have removed a real control on a false premise.\n\n  Wiring it into `verify_workflow_preflight` was **attempted and reverted**: that\n  preflight legitimately runs against synthetic workspaces where the workflow\n  YAMLs are absent, so the inventory verdict is invalid there for a reason that is\n  not a defect (`test_workflow_enterprise_preflight` proved it immediately). A\n  repo-wide invariant needs a caller that always sees the real repo. That entry\n  point — a CLI verb plus a CI step — is the open decision.\n\n- **`validate_file`** — waiver read as dead code (\"no CLI verb attached\"). It is\n  the ENGINE of the V4 narrative-shape invariant\n  (`tests/invariants/v4/test_phase_v4_b_narrative_shape.py:62,66`), which runs it\n  over every agent file. Its consumer is an invariant test, which is this\n  control's intended and sufficient consumer.\n\n- **`validate_request`** — WIRED (ARIA-HIGH-104, 2026-09-12; the waiver is gone).\n  The vocabularies are reconciled: the queue row `create_agent_invocation_request`\n  appends IS the `aria/agent-request/v1` envelope (`$schema` written from\n  `agent_contract.REQUEST_SCHEMA`; `forbidden_scope` and `validation_commands`\n  always written), and for every role in `agent_contract.CONTRACT_ENFORCED_ROLES`\n  the queue calls `validate_request(row, base_dir)` before the append. The set's\n  boundary is what the contract binds — a request to its cycle (`cycle_id`) and to\n  the plan revision it works from (`convergence_id` + `plan_revision_hash`); the\n  implementation role has both on every production mint. The operator-driven\n  judge, curation, questioning and self-change lanes run in no cycle and implement\n  no plan revision, so the contract has nothing to bind them to; their rows are\n  held by the queue's field validation at mint and `validate_response` at submit.\n  See CONTRACTS §12.17.\n\n### The classification problem, now seen three ways\n\n`CONTROL_VERBS` decides what the gate can see, and it is wrong in both\ndirections:\n\n|                     |                                                                     |\n| ------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n| `record_resolution` | a real control the prefix list **cannot see** (`ORPHAN-MEDIUM-808`) |\n\nThat rule is right for a control meant to run in production and wrong for one\nwhose consumer is the invariant itself. Teaching the gate that category is open\nwork; it is named here so the next reader does not rediscover it a fourth time.\n\n### Result\n\nExpired dormancy waivers **10 → 0**. `test_control_reachability` passes 8/8, and\nwith it the `aria-kernel` / `unittest` check that had been red on `main` since\n2026-09-07. 126 tests green across every module touched.\n\n## Open items a future reader should not re-derive\n\nMeasured 2026-09-09; check them before acting, they may have moved.\n\n| Control             | Disposition    | Why                                                                                        |\n| ------------------- | -------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |\n| `verify_branch_tip` | keep (in date) | `merge_pr_if_ready` does its own inline head-SHA comparison; PLAN Wave 8 collapses the two |\n\nWhat 2026-09-09 actually taught, now that the deletions have been done rather\nthan planned:\n\n**Five controls were nominated for deletion on the strength of their waivers.\nThree survived the proof and were deleted. Two did not** — one guarded something\nunduplicated (`verify_workflow_registry`) and one was not a control at all\n(`check_remaining_budget`). A sixth (`validate_file`) had a waiver that read as\ndead code and turned out to be an invariant's engine.\n\nSo the instinct \"at this stage the healthy move is usually removal\" is right\nabout the population and useless as a rule, because it is wrong about a third of\nthe individuals. The rule that survives is the ORDER:\n\n> Prove the live answer FIRST, by reading it, and only then delete. A waiver's\n> stated reason is a lead, not evidence — three of the ones checked today were\n> materially false, and each would have removed or hidden a real control if\n> followed.\n\nDeleting a proven duplicate reduces the surface the safety system has to keep\nconsistent with itself; it does not weaken the system and it is not \"silencing\"\nthe gate — the gate stays armed, with less to be wrong about. Deleting an\nUNPROVEN one is how a repository loses a control and keeps the green check.\n","numLines":446,"startLine":1,"totalLines":446}}}
