The CAPABILITY_REQUIRED flow it belongs to is PLAN Wave 6 (parent mission blocks, genesis child mission produces the capability, parent resumes); until that flow exists there is no caller with a capability to resolve.\"\n7\t  },\n8\t  \"require_lifecycle_state\": {\n9\t    \"expires_on\": \"2026-09-20\",\n10\t    \"finding_id\": \"ORPHAN-HIGH-573\",\n11\t    \"owner\": \"okan\",\n12\t    \"reason\": \"Genesis lifecycle state precondition. The genesis machine runs today through its own transition table; this helper is the alternative entry point. PLAN Wave 6 merges the genesis and tool_registry lifecycles onto one vocabulary - route it or drop it there, not before.\"\n13\t  },\n14\t  \"validate_file\": {\n15\t    \"expires_on\": \"2026-10-09\",\n16\t    \"finding_id\": \"ORPHAN-HIGH-573\",\n17\t    \"owner\": \"okan\",\n18\t    \"reason\": \"REASON REPLACED 2026-09-09. The previous one said 'Operator-facing CLI surface with no CLI verb attached - give it one or delete it', which reads as dead code. It is not. validate_file is the ENGINE of the V4 narrative-shape invariant: tests/invariants/v4/test_phase_v4_b_narrative_shape.py:62,66 runs it over every agent file to enforce the pedagogy contract, and that test is a real gate. pedagogy_lint.py (which does run in CI) only mentions this module in a comment; it does not import it. So the consumer is an invariant test, which is this control's INTENDED and sufficient consumer -- there is no unguarded production path here to close. The reachability gate cannot express that distinction: its rule that 'tests calling a control directly cannot count as evidence' is right for a control meant to run in production and wrong for one whose gate IS the invariant. Third instance of the CONTROL_VERBS classification problem, after record_resolution (a real control the prefix list cannot see) and check_remaining_budget (an accessor it wrongly called a control, since renamed). Open decision is whether the gate learns 'invariant-driven control' as a category, not whether to delete this.\"\n19\t  },\n20\t  \"validate_generated_adapter\": {\n21\t    \"expires_on\": \"2026-09-20\",\n22\t    \"finding_id\": \"ORPHAN-HIGH-573\",\n23\t    \"owner\": \"okan\",\n24\t    \"reason\": \"Skill-genesis adapter validation. The adapter authoring loop is dispatched through the convergent drafting agents, and the kernel-side validation of their output is not yet on that path - which is precisely the shape of defect this gate exists to surface, on a lane that is not live.\"\n25\t  },\n26\t  \"verify_adapter_imports\": {\n27\t    \"expires_on\": \"2026-09-20\",\n28\t    \"finding_id\": \"ORPHAN-HIGH-573\",\n29\t    \"owner\": \"okan\",\n30\t    \"reason\": \"Sandbox check for a generated adapter's import set. Same dormant skill-genesis lane; wire with its siblings or not at all.\"\n37\t  },\n38\t  \"verify_branch_tip\": {\n39\t    \"expires_on\": \"2026-09-20\",\n40\t    \"finding_id\": \"ORPHAN-HIGH-573\",\n41\t    \"owner\": \"okan\",\n42\t    \"reason\": \"Auto-merge head-SHA freshness check. merge_pr_if_ready performs its own head-SHA freshness comparison inline, so the protection exists; this is the extracted helper that the inline code never adopted. PLAN Wave 8 implements branch_tip_lock_and_recheck as a real hard-fail check - that is where these two collapse into one.\"\n43\t  },\n44\t  \"verify_workflow_registry\": {\n45\t    \"expires_on\": \"2026-10-09\",\n46\t    \"finding_id\": \"ORPHAN-HIGH-573\",\n47\t    \"owner\": \"okan\",\n48\t    \"reason\": \"REASON REPLACED 2026-09-09, and the previous one was FALSE. It claimed 'the equivalent assertion runs in CI as a TypeScript invariant, so the drift IS caught - by the other lane'. Checked: no spec under tests/invariants references WORKFLOW_CONTRACTS, and the nearest one (aria-kernel-workflow-setup.spec.ts) asserts something else entirely -- that a job running kernel code provisions the kernel first. Deleting on that reason would have removed an UNDUPLICATED guarantee: preflight's live verify_workflow_contract validates ONE contract against its YAML and by construction only ever visits workflows that already have a contract, so an ARIA workflow added with neither a contract nor an audited exclusion is invisible to it. Only the whole-inventory verdict sees that. Wiring it into verify_workflow_preflight was attempted and REVERTED: that preflight legitimately runs against synthetic workspaces, where the workflow YAMLs are absent and the inventory verdict is invalid for a reason that is not a defect (test_workflow_enterprise_preflight proved it). A repo-wide invariant needs a caller that always sees the real repo, and neither a CLI verb nor a CI step exists for it yet -- that entry point is the open decision, not whether the control is worth keeping. Verified valid=True against the live tree on 2026-09-09, so it will pass the day it is wired.\"\n49\t  }\n50\t}\n51\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"15e2d44a-5660-4a51-a46d-eca1518eadb6","uuid":"06c390c5-2475-4e83-9a69-d30029343115","timestamp":"2026-09-25T10:48:07.790Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-consensus-arbiter-00bb0e8ea72e/aria-kernel/control-reachability.dormant.json","content":"{\n  \"require_capability_resolution\": {\n    \"expires_on\": \"2026-09-20\",\n    \"finding_id\": \"ORPHAN-HIGH-573\",\n    \"owner\": \"okan\",\n    \"reason\": \"Refuses a mission step whose required capability is unresolved. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01DZmCQKu7rm9uqMsRT2Lamm","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"Which of ARIA's own controls are actually connected to anything.\n2\t\n3\tThis module exists because one defect keeps recurring in this repository\n4\tunder different names, and every instance was found by a human noticing\n5\trather than by a gate firing:\n6\t\n7\t    ORPHAN-CRITICAL-498  a perimeter with no production caller\n8\t    ORPHAN-HIGH-569      a project graph whose discovery list the repo outgrew\n9\t    ORPHAN-MEDIUM-571    a repository map nothing refreshed and nothing read\n10\t    ORPHAN-MEDIUM-572    a request vocabulary nothing consults\n11\t\n12\tThey are one defect: **a control that is correct, tested, exported — and\n13\tcalled by nobody.** A green suite says nothing about it, because the tests\n14\tcall it directly; that is precisely how it stays green while governing\n15\tnothing.\n16\t\n17\t`tests/invariants/invariant-reachability.spec.ts` already proved the cure for\n18\tTypeScript invariant specs: enumerate what should run, require a declared\n19\twaiver for anything that does not, and make the waiver's expiry load-bearing\n20\tagainst the clock. That spec learned the hard way that validating the SHAPE\n21\tof an expiry date instead of the date lets 25 waivers pass their deadline\n22\ttogether in silence. This module applies the same discipline to the kernel's\n23\town control surface, deliberately reusing the proven mechanism rather than\n24\tinventing a second one.\n25\t\n26\tWHAT COUNTS AS A CONTROL. A public, module-level callable in ``aria_kernel``\n27\twhose name begins with a control verb — ``validate_``, ``enforce_``,\n28\t``assert_``, ``require_``, ``verify_``, ``guard_``, ``refuse_``, ``check_``.\n29\tThis is a naming convention the kernel already follows consistently, which is\n30\twhat makes it a usable definition rather than an arbitrary one: it selects 85\n31\tfunctions today, and it selects them because their authors named them as\n32\tguarantees.\n33\t\n34\tWHAT COUNTS AS REACHABLE, and where the bar sits. A control is reachable if a\n35\tproduction module (not a test) USES it: a call, a bare name in a registry\n36\ttuple, a string in a dispatch table. The bar is deliberately generous about\n37\tindirection, because a control wired through a registry must not be falsely\n38\taccused — a gate that cries wolf is a gate that gets waived into uselessness.\n39\t\n40\tFour things are excluded, and each exclusion is a lesson rather than a\n41\tpreference:\n42\t\n43\t* its own ``def`` line — defining is not calling;\n44\t* its module's ``__all__`` — exporting is not calling, and ORPHAN-MEDIUM-572\n45\t  was in ``__all__`` while governing nothing;\n46\t* prose — a docstring naming a sibling check is how\n47\t  ``verify_no_secret_in_envelope`` looked connected;\n48\t* **imports** — importing is not calling. This one was found by mutating the\n49\t  gate itself; see ``_import_lines``.\n50\t\n51\tWHAT THIS GATE DOES NOT MEASURE, stated because a green gate must not be read\n52\tas more than it is. It asks *\"is this control called\"*. It does **not** ask\n53\t*\"can this control's refusal branch be reached with production inputs\"* — and\n54\tthose are different questions with different answers.\n55\t\n56\tThe live counter-example is ``ORPHAN-HIGH-577``:\n57\t``agent_contract.enforce_separation_of_duties`` is invoked on every agent\n58\tsubmit, so it is reachable and this gate passes it, yet the field its refusal\n59\tdepends on (``separation_of_duties.forbidden_agent_ids``) has no production\n60\twriter, so it always evaluates an empty list and can never refuse. A control\n61\tthat is called and cannot decide is the same false assurance as one that is\n62\tnever called — and it is invisible here.\n63\t\n64\tA generalised efficacy check was prototyped and deliberately not shipped: a\n65\tmechanical scan for controls reading dict keys that no production module\n66\twrites flagged 10 of 68 live controls, but most were false positives from\n67\twriters living in JSON/YAML or from short key names. A gate that cries wolf\n68\tgets waived into uselessness, which would cost more than the gap it closes.\n69\tUntil that check can be made precise, efficacy is tracked per finding rather\n70\tthan asserted here.\n71\t\"\"\"\n72\t\n73\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n74\t\n75\timport ast\n76\timport re\n77\tfrom pathlib import Path\n78\tfrom typing import Any\n79\t\n80\tCONTROL_VERBS = (\n81\t    \"validate_\",\n82\t    \"enforce_\",\n83\t    \"assert_\",\n84\t    \"require_\",\n85\t    \"verify_\",\n86\t    \"guard_\",\n87\t    \"refuse_\",\n88\t    \"check_\",\n89\t)\n90\t\n91\t# Directories whose Python is not production: the tests that call a control\n92\t# directly are exactly what cannot count as evidence that anything else does.\n93\t_NON_PRODUCTION_PARTS = (\"tests\", \"node_modules\", \".git\", \"__pycache__\", \"build\", \"dist\")\n94\t\n95\t# The repository's production Python lives in exactly these roots (measured:\n96\t# aria-kernel 640 files, tools 30, scripts 2). Scanning only them keeps the\n97\t# walk ~16x smaller and, more importantly, keeps it OUT of the transient\n98\t# directories the rest of the suite creates while this gate is running — a\n99\t# gate that flakes is a gate that gets ignored, which for an anti-defect gate\n100\t# is the worst outcome available.\n101\t#\n102\t# A hardcoded roster is the same shape as the defect ORPHAN-HIGH-569 was: a\n103\t# list that was true when written and silently stopped describing the repo. So\n104\t# it does NOT stand alone — `unrostered_production_dirs` reports any directory\n105\t# holding production Python outside it, and the suite fails on a non-empty\n106\t# result. The list may be wrong; it cannot be wrong quietly.\n107\tPRODUCTION_SOURCE_ROOTS = (\"aria-kernel\", \"tools\", \"scripts\")\n108\t\n109\t\n110\tdef kernel_root(repo_root: str | Path) -> Path:\n111\t    return Path(repo_root) / \"aria-kernel\" / \"aria_kernel\"\n112\t\n113\t\n114\tdef declared_controls(repo_root: str | Path) -> dict[str, dict[str, Any]]:\n115\t    \"\"\"Every public control-verb callable in the kernel, by name.\"\"\"\n116\t    root = kernel_root(repo_root)\n117\t    controls: dict[str, dict[str, Any]] = {}\n118\t    for path in sorted(root.rglob(\"*.py\")):\n119\t        try:\n120\t            tree = ast.parse(path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n121\t        except (OSError, SyntaxError):\n122\t            continue\n123\t        module = path.relative_to(root).as_posix()\n124\t        for node in tree.body:\n125\t            if not isinstance(node, (ast.FunctionDef, ast.AsyncFunctionDef)):\n126\t                continue\n127\t node.lineno}\n130\t    return controls\n131\t\n132\t\n133\tdef _all_block(path: Path) -> tuple[int, int]:\n134\t    \"\"\"Line span of a module's ``__all__``, or (0, 0).\"\"\"\n135\t    try:\n136\t ast.Assign) and any(\n141\t            getattr(target, \"id\", \"\") == \"__all__\" for target in node.targets\n142\t        ):\n143\t            return (node.lineno, node.end_lineno or node.lineno)\n144\t    return (0, 0)\n145\t\n146\t\n147\tdef _import_lines(source: str) -> set[int]:\n148\t    \"\"\"Every line occupied by an import statement.\n149\t\n150\t    An import is NOT a use, and the distinction is load-bearing rather than\n151\t    pedantic: this gate's own mutation check — replace the one call to\n152\t    ``verify_no_secret_in_envelope`` with ``pass`` and leave the import —\n153\t    passed until imports stopped counting. A control that is imported and\n154\t    never invoked is exactly the defect being hunted, so counting the import\n155\t    would have let the gate certify it.\n156\t    \"\"\"\n157\t    try:\n158\t        tree = ast.parse(source)\n159\t    except SyntaxError:\n160\t        return set()\n161\t    spans: set[int] = set()\n162\t    for node in ast.walk(tree):\n163\t        if isinstance(node, (ast.Import, ast.ImportFrom)):\n164\t            spans.update(range(node.lineno, (node.end_lineno or node.lineno) + 1))\n165\t    return spans\n166\t\n167\t\n168\tdef _is_production_python(path: Path) -> bool:\n169\t    if any(part in _NON_PRODUCTION_PARTS for part in path.parts):\n170\t        return False\n171\t    return not (path.name.startswith(\"test_\") or path.name.endswith(\"_test.py\"))\n172\t\n173\t\n174\tdef _walk_python(root: Path) -> list[Path]:\n175\t    \"\"\"``rglob`` that cannot be broken by a directory vanishing mid-walk.\n176\t\n177\t    The suite this gate runs inside creates and removes trees while it runs.\n178\t    An unguarded walk over live filesystem state is a coin flip, and a gate\n179\t    that fails once in four runs teaches people to re-run it rather than to\n180\t    read it.\n181\t    \"\"\"\n182\t    try:\n183\t        return sorted(p for p in root.rglob(\"*.py\") if p.is_file())\n184\t    except OSError:\n185\t        return []\n186\t\n187\t\n188\tdef unrostered_production_dirs(repo_root: str | Path) -> dict[str, int]:\n189\t    \"\"\"Top-level directories holding production Python outside the roster.\n190\t\n191\t    The completeness check that keeps PRODUCTION_SOURCE_ROOTS from becoming\n192\t    the next stale hardcoded list.\n193\t    \"\"\"\n194\t    root = Path(repo_root)\n195\t    stray: dict[str, int] = {}\n196\t    for path in _walk_python(root):\n197\t        try:\n198\t            relative = path.relative_to(root)\n199\t        except ValueError:\n200\t            continue\n201\t        parts = relative.parts\n202\t        # Nested git worktrees under .claude/worktrees are CHECKOUTS of this\n203\t        # same repository (parallel sessions each get one), every one carrying\n204\t        # a full kernel copy. Counting them would make the roster test fail on\n205\t        # any active session and pass only when the machine is idle — a gate\n206\t        # that depends on machine activity measures the machine, not the repo.\n207\t        # The roster's question is \"where does THIS tree's production code\n208\t        # live\", and a checkout of this tree at another commit is not an\n209\t        # answer to it.\n210\t        if len(parts) >= 2 and parts[0] == \".claude\" and parts[1] == \"worktrees\":\n211\t            continue\n212\t        # Same class, different checkout location: .worktrees/ holds\n213\t        # parallel-session worktrees (the promotion-chain branch's session\n214\t        # uses this root). A checkout of this tree at another commit is\n215\t        # not an answer to the roster's question, regardless of where the\n216\t        # worktree happens to be mounted.\n217\t        if parts[0] == \".worktrees\":\n218\t            continue\n219\t        top = parts[0] if len(parts) > 1 else \".\"\n220\t        if top in PRODUCTION_SOURCE_ROOTS or not _is_production_python(relative):\n221\t            continue\n222\t        stray[top] = stray.get(top, 0) + 1\n223\t    return stray\n224\t\n225\t\n226\tdef production_sources(repo_root: str | Path) -> list[Path]:\n227\t    \"\"\"Every production Python file under the roster.\n228\t\n229\t    The single definition of \"production Python\" in the kernel. It is public\n230\t    because `surface_reachability` asks the same question about the same\n231\t    files, and two independently-drifting answers to *\"what counts as\n232\t    production\"* would let a caller be production for one gate and invisible\n233\t    to the other.\n234\t    \"\"\"\n235\t    root = Path(repo_root)\n236\t    files: list[Path] = []\n237\t    for source_root in PRODUCTION_SOURCE_ROOTS:\n238\t        for path in _walk_python(root / source_root):\n239\t            if _is_production_python(path):\n240\t                files.append(path)\n241\t    return files\n242\t\n243\t\n244\tdef _production_lines(repo_root: str | Path) -> dict[Path, tuple[list[str], set[int]]]:\n245\t    sources: dict[Path, tuple[list[str], set[int]]] = {}\n246\t    for path in production_sources(repo_root):\n247\t        try:\n248\t            text = path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n249\t        except OSError:\n250\t            continue\n251\t        sources[path] = (text.splitlines(), _import_lines(text))\n252\t    return sources\n253\t\n254\t\n255\tdef first_production_reference(\n256\t    name: str,\n257\t    control: dict[str, Any],\n258\t    *,\n259\t    repo_root: str | Path,\n260\t    sources: dict[Path, tuple[list[str], set[int]]] | None = None,\n261\t) -> dict[str, Any] | None:\n262\t    \"\"\"Where production first USES this control, or None if nowhere.\n263\t\n264\t    Uses, not mentions: the definition, the module's ``__all__``, any import\n265\t    statement, and prose are all excluded, because none of them causes the\n266\t    control to run.\n267\t    \"\"\"\n268\t    root = kernel_root(repo_root)\n269\t    owner = root / control[\"module\"]\n270\t    all_lo, all_hi = _all_block(owner)\n271\t    pattern = re.compile(rf\"\\b{re.escape(name)}\\b\")\n272\t    for path, (lines, import_lines) in (sources or _production_lines(repo_root)).items():\n273\t        is_owner = path.resolve() == owner.resolve()\n274\t        for lineno, line in enumerate(lines, 1):\n275\t            if not pattern.search(line):\n276\t                continue\n277\t            if lineno in import_lines:\n278\t                continue\n279\t            if is_owner and (lineno == control[\"lineno\"] or all_lo <= lineno <= all_hi):\n280\t                continue\n281\t            stripped = line.lstrip()\n282\t            # Prose mentions are not wiring. A docstring naming a sibling\n283\t            # check is how `verify_no_secret_in_envelope` looked reachable.\n284\t            if stripped.startswith((\"#\", '\"\"\"', \"'''\", \"*\")) or \"``\" in line:\n285\t                continue\n286\t            return {\n287\t                \"path\": Path(path).relative_to(Path(repo_root)).as_posix(),\n288\t                \"lineno\": lineno,\n289\t                \"line\": stripped[:120],\n290\t            }\n291\t    return None\n292\t\n293\t\n294\tdef unreachable_controls(repo_root: str | Path) -> dict[str, dict[str, Any]]:\n295\t    \"\"\"Controls nothing in production refers to — the dormant surface.\"\"\"\n296\t    sources = _production_lines(repo_root)\n297\t    controls = declared_controls(repo_root)\n298\t    return {\n299\t        name: control\n300\t        for name, control in controls.items()\n301\t        if first_production_reference(name, control, repo_root=repo_root, sources=sources)\n302\t        is None\n303\t of ARIA's own controls are actually connected to anything.\n\nThis module exists because one defect keeps recurring in this repository\nunder different names, and every instance was found by a human noticing\nrather than by a gate firing:\n\n    ORPHAN-CRITICAL-498  a perimeter with no production caller\n    ORPHAN-HIGH-569      a project graph whose discovery list the repo outgrew\n    ORPHAN-MEDIUM-571    a repository map nothing refreshed and nothing read\n    ORPHAN-MEDIUM-572    a request vocabulary nothing consults\n\nThey are one defect: **a control that is correct, tested, exported — and\ncalled by nobody.** A green suite says nothing about it, because the tests\ncall it directly; that is precisely how it stays green while governing\nnothing.\n\n`tests/invariants/invariant-reachability.spec.ts` already proved the cure for\nTypeScript invariant specs: enumerate what should run, require a declared\nwaiver for anything that does not, and make the waiver's expiry load-bearing\nagainst the clock. That spec learned the hard way that validating the SHAPE\nof an expiry date instead of the date lets 25 waivers pass their deadline\ntogether in silence. This module applies the same discipline to the kernel's\nown control surface, deliberately reusing the proven mechanism rather than\ninventing a second one.\n\nWHAT COUNTS AS A CONTROL. A public, module-level callable in ``aria_kernel``\nwhose name begins with a control verb — ``validate_``, ``enforce_``,\n``assert_``, ``require_``, ``verify_``, ``guard_``, ``refuse_``, ``check_``.\nThis is a naming convention the kernel already follows consistently, which is\nwhat makes it a usable definition rather than an arbitrary one: it selects 85\nfunctions today, and it selects them because their authors named them as\nguarantees.\n\nWHAT COUNTS AS REACHABLE, and where the bar sits. A control is reachable if a\nproduction module (not a test) USES it: a call, a bare name in a registry\ntuple, a string in a dispatch table. The bar is deliberately generous about\nindirection, because a control wired through a registry must not be falsely\naccused — a gate that cries wolf is a gate that gets waived into uselessness.\n\nFour things are excluded, and each exclusion is a lesson rather than a\npreference:\n\n* its own ``def`` line — defining is not calling;\n* its module's ``__all__`` — exporting is not calling, and ORPHAN-MEDIUM-572\n  was in ``__all__`` while governing nothing;\n* prose — a docstring naming a sibling check is how\n  ``verify_no_secret_in_envelope`` looked connected;\n* **imports** — importing is not calling. This one was found by mutating the\n  gate itself; see ``_import_lines``.\n\nWHAT THIS GATE DOES NOT MEASURE, stated because a green gate must not be read\nas more than it is. It asks *\"is this control called\"*. It does **not** ask\n*\"can this control's refusal branch be reached with production inputs\"* — and\nthose are different questions with different answers.\n\nThe live counter-example is ``ORPHAN-HIGH-577``:\n``agent_contract.enforce_separation_of_duties`` is invoked on every agent\nsubmit, so it is reachable and this gate passes it, yet the field its refusal\ndepends on (``separation_of_duties.forbidden_agent_ids``) has no production\nwriter, so it always evaluates an empty list and can never refuse. A control\nthat is called and cannot decide is the same false assurance as one that is\nnever called — and it is invisible here.\n\nA generalised efficacy check was prototyped and deliberately not shipped: a\nmechanical scan for controls reading dict keys that no production module\nwrites flagged 10 of 68 live controls, but most were false positives from\nwriters living in JSON/YAML or from short key names. A gate that cries wolf\ngets waived into uselessness, which would cost more than the gap it closes.\nUntil that check can be made precise, efficacy is tracked per finding rather\nthan asserted here.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport ast\nimport re\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import Any\n\nCONTROL_VERBS = (\n    \"validate_\",\n    \"enforce_\",\n    \"assert_\",\n    \"require_\",\n    \"verify_\",\n    \"guard_\",\n    \"refuse_\",\n    \"check_\",\n)\n\n# Directories whose Python is not production: the tests that call a control\n# directly are exactly what cannot count as evidence that anything else does.\n_NON_PRODUCTION_PARTS = (\"tests\", \"node_modules\", \".git\", \"__pycache__\", \"build\", \"dist\")\n\n# The repository's production Python lives in exactly these roots (measured:\n# aria-kernel 640 files, tools 30, scripts 2). Scanning only them keeps the\n# walk ~16x smaller and, more importantly, keeps it OUT of the transient\n# directories the rest of the suite creates while this gate is running — a\n# gate that flakes is a gate that gets ignored, which for an anti-defect gate\n# is the worst outcome available.\n#\n# A hardcoded roster is the same shape as the defect ORPHAN-HIGH-569 was: a\n# list that was true when written and silently stopped describing the repo. So\n# it does NOT stand alone — `unrostered_production_dirs` reports any directory\n# holding production Python outside it, and the suite fails on a non-empty\n# result. The list may be wrong; it cannot be wrong quietly.\nPRODUCTION_SOURCE_ROOTS = (\"aria-kernel\", \"tools\", \"scripts\")\n\n\ndef kernel_root(repo_root: str | Path) -> Path:\n    return Path(repo_root) / \"aria-kernel\" / \"aria_kernel\"\n\n\ndef declared_controls(repo_root: str | Path) -> dict[str, dict[str, Any]]:\n    \"\"\"Every public control-verb callable in the kernel, by name.\"\"\"\n    root = kernel_root(repo_root)\n    controls: dict[str, dict[str, Any]] = {}\n    for path in sorted(root.rglob(\"*.py\")):\n        try:\n            tree = ast.parse(path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n        except (OSError, SyntaxError):\n            continue\n        module = path.relative_to(root).as_posix()\n        for node in tree.body:\n            if not isinstance(node, (ast.FunctionDef, ast.AsyncFunctionDef)):\n                continue\n            if node.name.startswith(\"_\") or not node.name.startswith(CONTROL_VERBS):\n                continue\n            controls[node.name] = {\"module\": module, \"lineno\": node.lineno}\n    return controls\n\n\ndef _all_block(path: Path) -> tuple[int, int]:\n    \"\"\"Line span of a module's ``__all__``, or (0, 0).\"\"\"\n    try:\n        tree = ast.parse(path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n    except (OSError, SyntaxError):\n        return (0, 0)\n    for node in tree.body:\n        if isinstance(node, ast.Assign) and any(\n            getattr(target, \"id\", \"\") == \"__all__\" for target in node.targets\n        ):\n            return (node.lineno, node.end_lineno or node.lineno)\n    return (0, 0)\n\n\ndef _import_lines(source: str) -> set[int]:\n    \"\"\"Every line occupied by an import statement.\n\n    An import is NOT a use, and the distinction is load-bearing rather than\n    pedantic: this gate's own mutation check — replace the one call to\n    ``verify_no_secret_in_envelope`` with ``pass`` and leave the import —\n    passed until imports stopped counting. A control that is imported and\n    never invoked is exactly the defect being hunted, so counting the import\n    would have let the gate certify it.\n    \"\"\"\n    try:\n        tree = ast.parse(source)\n    except SyntaxError:\n        return set()\n    spans: set[int] = set()\n    for node in ast.walk(tree):\n        if isinstance(node, (ast.Import, ast.ImportFrom)):\n            spans.update(range(node.lineno, (node.end_lineno or node.lineno) + 1))\n    return spans\n\n\ndef _is_production_python(path: Path) -> bool:\n    if any(part in _NON_PRODUCTION_PARTS for part in path.parts):\n        return False\n    return not (path.name.startswith(\"test_\") or path.name.endswith(\"_test.py\"))\n\n\ndef _walk_python(root: Path) -> list[Path]:\n    \"\"\"``rglob`` that cannot be broken by a directory vanishing mid-walk.\n\n    The suite this gate runs inside creates and removes trees while it runs.\n    An unguarded walk over live filesystem state is a coin flip, and a gate\n    that fails once in four runs teaches people to re-run it rather than to\n    read it.\n    \"\"\"\n    try:\n        return sorted(p for p in root.rglob(\"*.py\") if p.is_file())\n    except OSError:\n        return []\n\n\ndef unrostered_production_dirs(repo_root: str | Path) -> dict[str, int]:\n    \"\"\"Top-level directories holding production Python outside the roster.\n\n    The completeness check that keeps PRODUCTION_SOURCE_ROOTS from becoming\n    the next stale hardcoded list.\n    \"\"\"\n    root = Path(repo_root)\n    stray: dict[str, int] = {}\n    for path in _walk_python(root):\n        try:\n            relative = path.relative_to(root)\n        except ValueError:\n            continue\n        parts = relative.parts\n        # Nested git worktrees under .claude/worktrees are CHECKOUTS of this\n        # same repository (parallel sessions each get one), every one carrying\n        # a full kernel copy. Counting them would make the roster test fail on\n        # any active session and pass only when the machine is idle — a gate\n        # that depends on machine activity measures the machine, not the repo.\n        # The roster's question is \"where does THIS tree's production code\n        # live\", and a checkout of this tree at another commit is not an\n        # answer to it.\n        if len(parts) >= 2 and parts[0] == \".claude\" and parts[1] == \"worktrees\":\n            continue\n        # Same class, different checkout location: .worktrees/ holds\n        # parallel-session worktrees (the promotion-chain branch's session\n        # uses this root). A checkout of this tree at another commit is\n        # not an answer to the roster's question, regardless of where the\n        # worktree happens to be mounted.\n        if parts[0] == \".worktrees\":\n            continue\n        top = parts[0] if len(parts) > 1 else \".\"\n        if top in PRODUCTION_SOURCE_ROOTS or not _is_production_python(relative):\n            continue\n        stray[top] = stray.get(top, 0) + 1\n    return stray\n\n\ndef production_sources(repo_root: str | Path) -> list[Path]:\n    \"\"\"Every production Python file under the roster.\n\n    The single definition of \"production Python\" in the kernel. It is public\n    because `surface_reachability` asks the same question about the same\n    files, and two independently-drifting answers to *\"what counts as\n    production\"* would let a caller be production for one gate and invisible\n    to the other.\n    \"\"\"\n    root = Path(repo_root)\n    files: list[Path] = []\n    for source_root in PRODUCTION_SOURCE_ROOTS:\n        for path in _walk_python(root / source_root):\n            if _is_production_python(path):\n                files.append(path)\n    return files\n\n\ndef _production_lines(repo_root: str | Path) -> dict[Path, tuple[list[str], set[int]]]:\n    sources: dict[Path, tuple[list[str], set[int]]] = {}\n    for path in production_sources(repo_root):\n        try:\n            text = path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n        except OSError:\n            continue\n        sources[path] = (text.splitlines(), _import_lines(text))\n    return sources\n\n\ndef      for lineno, line in enumerate(lines, 1):\n            if not pattern.search(line):\n                continue\n            if lineno in import_lines:\n                continue\n            if is_owner and (lineno == control[\"lineno\"] or all_lo <= lineno <= all_hi):\n                continue\n            stripped = line.lstrip()\n            # Prose mentions are not wiring. A docstring naming a sibling\n            # check is how `verify_no_secret_in_envelope` looked reachable.\n            if stripped.startswith((\"#\", '\"\"\"', \"'''\", \"*\")) or \"``\" in line:\n                continue\n            return {\n                \"path\": Path(path).relative_to(Path(repo_root)).as_posix(),\n                \"lineno\": lineno,\n                \"line\": stripped[:120],\n            }\n    return None\n\n\ndef unreachable_controls(repo_root: str | Path) -> dict[str, dict[str, Any]]:\n    \"\"\"Controls nothing in production refers to — the dormant surface.\"\"\"\n    sources = _production_lines(repo_root)\n    controls = declared_controls(repo_root)\n    return {\n        name: control\n        for name, control in controls.items()\n        if first_production_reference(name, control, repo_root=repo_root, sources=sources)\n        is None\n    }\n\n\n__all__ = [\n    \"CONTROL_VERBS\",\n    \"declared_controls\",\n    \"first_production_reference\",\n    \"kernel_root\",\n    \"PRODUCTION_SOURCE_ROOTS\",\n    \"production_sources\",\n    \"unrostered_production_dirs\",\n    \"unreachable_controls\",\n]\n","numLines":316,"startLine":1,"totalLines":316}}}

Direct API-key / proxy-billing runtime mode is not the default\n80\tauthority for this repository.\n81\t\n82\tLegacy Codex executor language in older docs is historical or compatibility reference unless an\n83\texecutable contract explicitly calls it. Any live doc section that treats `codex exec`,\n84\tChatGPT-managed Codex auth, or `codex_runtime.py` as the current ARIA runtime authority is a\n85\tdocumentation defect.\n86\t\n87\t## Execution Policy Amendment (2026-09-11)\n88\t\n89\tThe preceding mainline runtime description is an executable-history boundary, not proof that\n90\tthe candidate is deployed. The current user-required adaptive design has these distinct routes:\n91\t\n92\t| Provider  | Required transport/authentication                                                                                                              | Current proof boundary                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |\n93\t| --------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |\n94\t| OpenAI    | Actual Codex CLI with managed ChatGPT/subscription login; request Astra Ultra. No API key or direct API fallback.                              | Integrated offline profile/argv and filesystem-probe controls; effective managed authentication and native dispatch remain open.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |\n95\t| Anthropic | Actual Claude Code CLI with managed subscription login. No Console/API-key/cloud or direct API fallback.                                       | Natively admitted since 2026-09-11 on `claude auth status --json` (claude.ai = subscription; API-key/console logins refused by name) and executed through the existing containment spawn with attempt/finished evidence (`_invoke_native_claude`, `test_ci_executor_native_claude`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |\n96\t| Z.ai      | Separate API transport with its own scoped credential and explicit product/endpoint/model identity. Never redirect either managed CLI to Z.ai. | `tools/aria-poc/zai_runtime.py` (OpenAI-compatible chat completions over `urllib`) is wired into fleet availability, native admission (`observe_status` probe), native execution (`_invoke_native_zai`) and the legacy ladder (`_run_zai_as_claude_result`); proven end to end against a local stand-in vendor (`test_ci_executor_native_zai`). Live on 2026-09-11: Coding-Plan probe HTTP 200 (general route 429/1113 — wallet, not plan); the first ACCEPTED native planner result in ARIA ran on this route (trial six, `AIR-aria-challenger-planner-b82f89291809`, glm-5.3, state `CHALLENGER_DRAFTED`). |\n97\t\n98\tThe user's Z.ai credential has not been provisioned to the reviewed runtime. Its boundary is\n99\t`ARIA_ZAI_API_KEY_FILE`: an absolute path to a regular file readable by its owner only\n100\t(mode `0600`; any group/world bit is refused by name), holding exactly one line — or, for\n101\tCI-secret injection only, `ARIA_ZAI_API_KEY`; both at once is refused as ambiguous\n102\t(`zai_runtime.read_zai_credential`). The value is held in a `ZaiCredential` whose repr,\n103\tstr and equality never expose it, is used only to build one `Authorization: Bearer` header,\n104\tand is never placed in `os.environ`, argv, a ledger row, a governance event, an exception\n105\tmessage or a transcript; `agent_env.SECRET_SHAPED_ENV_NAME` drops both variable names from\n106\tevery agent child. The former `claude_runtime.provider_redirect_env` route — the `claude`\n107\tbinary with `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` pointed at Z.ai — is deleted, and `run_claude_exec` refuses\n108\ta non-Anthropic model by name (`model_not_served_by_claude_runtime`). No credential value,\n109\tdigest or fragment belongs in source, command arguments, evidence or documentation.\n110\t\n111\tThe Z.ai general API and Coding Plan endpoints are distinct and both are named in\n112\t`zai_runtime.ZAI_ENDPOINTS` (`coding` → `/api/coding/paas/v4`, the subscription quota;\n113\t`general` → `/api/paas/v4`, the prepaid wallet); the vendor's GLM-5.3 page states that\n114\tCoding-Plan subscribers reach the model API only through the OpenAI-compatible protocol, so\n115\t`coding` is the default and `ARIA_ZAI_ENDPOINT` selects otherwise (a documented name, or an\n116\texplicit base URL recorded as `custom`). Entitlement is never inferred from key shape or\n117\tsilently substituted: `probe_zai_status` makes one `max_tokens=1` completion against the\n118\tselected endpoint and records the HTTP status, the vendor's error code and message, and the\n119\t(auth, quota) classification as the candidate observation the fleet admission reads. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01H9i7aTa6ZPvxmYnpScfmKK","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"Single source of truth for ARIA agent roles and lifecycle labels.\n2\t\n3\tThe runtime has several consumers that need the same closed sets:\n4\tstrict request validation, invocation queue writes, bridge replay,\n5\tdispatcher polling, and lifecycle reducers.  Keeping those sets here\n6\tprevents the common failure mode where a new role is added to one\n7\tsurface but remains unreachable or unbridged elsewhere.\n8\t\"\"\"\n9\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n10\t\n11\tfrom typing import FrozenSet\n12\t\n13\t\n14\tREQUEST_ROLES: tuple[str, ...] = (\n15\t    \"primary_plan\",\n16\t    \"challenger_plan\",\n17\t    \"cross_review\",\n18\t    # Coverage-waiver adjudicator (plan-coverage gate PR-2). Deliberately\n19\t    # NOT in PLANNER_BRIDGE_ROLES: its result is annotation-only — the\n20\t    # drainer reads it from the invocation results ledger and folds the\n21\t    # verdict into the coverage_computed payload; no plan-state mutation\n22\t    # happens on submit.\n23\t    \"completeness_critique\",\n24\t    \"implementation\",\n25\t    \"verification\",\n26\t    \"gap_finding\",\n27\t    \"gap_closure\",\n28\t    \"maintenance_utility\",\n29\t    \"primary_authoring\",\n30\t    \"challenger_authoring\",\n31\t    \"evidence_judgment\",\n32\t    \"adversarial_judgment\",\n33\t    \"consensus_arbitration\",\n34\t    \"change_intelligence\",\n35\t    \"goldset_curation\",\n36\t    # E14 — REMOVED, and deliberately not replaced: `implementation_review`,\n37\t    # `architectural_arbitration`, `auth_security_review`,\n38\t    # `access_boundary_review`, `tenant_isolation_review`. Plan 019 Phase 2.5\n39\t    # added them as a contract extension for lanes that were then built\n40\t    # differently: the auth lane became the security-boundary ADAPTER plus the\n41\t    # architecture spine gate, and domain review became `specialist_domain_review`\n42\t    # (specialist_review_runner's touch-map) plus expert_review_gate. Five roles\n43\t    # a request could name, that no kernel path ever minted and no bridge ever\n44\t    # consumed — a surface that admits a role nothing can fulfil is how a caller\n45\t    # ends up waiting forever for an answer that was never dispatched. Removal is\n46\t    # pinned by tests/test_role_hygiene_e14.py; ORPHAN-MEDIUM-280 closed the same\n47\t    # defect from the agent-prompt side (a prompt claiming `implementation_review`\n48\t    # the kernel never routes).\n49\t    # ORPHAN-HIGH-426 — independent adjudication of a HUMAN_REQUIRED\n50\t    # escalation. Paired with THREE distinct judge agents below so a panel\n51\t    # is composed of distinct principals by construction rather than by\n52\t    # hope; the fold still verifies disjointness against the claims ledger.\n53\t    \"human_required_adjudication\",\n54\t)\n55\t\n56\tINVOCATION_ROLES: FrozenSet[str] = frozenset({\n57\t    *REQUEST_ROLES,\n58\t    \"specialist_domain_review\",\n59\t})\n60\t\n61\tDISPATCHABLE_ROLES: FrozenSet[str] = frozenset({\n62\t    \"specialist_domain_review\",\n63\t    \"primary_authoring\",\n64\t    \"challenger_authoring\",\n65\t    \"evidence_judgment\",\n66\t    \"adversarial_judgment\",\n67\t    \"primary_plan\",\n68\t    \"challenger_plan\",\n69\t    \"cross_review\",\n70\t    \"completeness_critique\",\n71\t    \"implementation\",\n72\t    \"human_required_adjudication\",\n73\t    # E14 — the three roles that gained a producer. A minted envelope whose\n74\t    # role the executor refuses to claim (`ci_executor.claim_and_dispatch_one`\n75\t    # validates against this set) is a request that waits forever: the mint\n76\t    # would look alive in the ledger and be dead on the lane. Minting and\n77\t    # draining are one contract, so a role joins both sides together.\n78\t    \"consensus_arbitration\",\n79\t    \"change_intelligence\",\n80\t    \"goldset_curation\",\n81\t    # E9-c — adversarial re-review of a decision the pipeline already closed.\n82\t    # Added together with the minter in `decision_questioning`, never before\n83\t    # it: a role that can be dispatched but is never minted, and a role that\n84\t    # is minted but can never be claimed, are the same defect from opposite\n85\t    # ends, and this repository has now closed nineteen of them.\n86\t    \"verification\",\n87\t})\n88\t\n89\tDRAFTER_ROLES: FrozenSet[str] = frozenset({\n90\t    \"primary_authoring\",\n91\t    \"challenger_authoring\",\n92\t})\n93\t\n94\tJUDGE_ROLES: tuple[str, ...] = (\n95\t    \"evidence_judgment\",\n96\t    \"adversarial_judgment\",\n97\t    \"consensus_arbitration\",\n98\t)\n99\t\n100\tSUPPORTING_ROLES: tuple[str, ...] = (\n101\t    \"change_intelligence\",\n102\t    \"goldset_curation\",\n103\t)\n104\t\n105\tPLANNER_BRIDGE_ROLES: FrozenSet[str] = frozenset({\n106\t    \"primary_plan\",\n107\t    \"challenger_plan\",\n108\t    \"cross_review\",\n109\t    \"implementation\",\n110\t})\n111\t\n112\tBRIDGE_REQUIRED_ROLES: FrozenSet[str] = frozenset({\n113\t    *JUDGE_ROLES,\n114\t    *SUPPORTING_ROLES,\n115\t    *PLANNER_BRIDGE_ROLES,\n116\t})\n117\t\n118\tDEFAULT_TARGET_AGENT_WHITELIST: tuple[str, ...] = (\n119\t    \"aria-prompt-writer\",\n120\t    \"aria-primary-planner\","}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"15e2d44a-5660-4a51-a46d-eca1518eadb6","uuid":"e27bcf81-fa06-4f8a-bb86-e2248f06f13e","timestamp":"2026-09-25T10:49:21.177Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-consensus-arbiter-00bb0e8ea72e/aria-kernel/aria_kernel/agent_surface.py","content":"\"\"\"Single source of truth for ARIA agent roles and lifecycle labels.\n\nThe runtime has several consumers that need the same closed sets:\nstrict request validation, invocation queue writes, bridge replay,\ndispatcher polling, and lifecycle reducers.  Keeping those sets here\nprevents the common failure mode where a new role is added to one\nsurface but remains unreachable or unbridged elsewhere.\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom typing import FrozenSet\n\n\nREQUEST_ROLES: tuple[str, ...] = (\n    \"primary_plan\",\n    \"challenger_plan\",\n    \"cross_review\",\n    # Coverage-waiver adjudicator (plan-coverage gate PR-2). Deliberately\n    # NOT in PLANNER_BRIDGE_ROLES: its result is annotation-only — the\n    # drainer reads it from the invocation results ledger and folds the\n    # verdict into the coverage_computed payload; no plan-state mutation\n    # happens on submit.\n    \"completeness_critique\",\n    \"implementation\",\n    \"verification\",\n    \"gap_finding\",\n    \"gap_closure\",\n    \"maintenance_utility\",\n    \"primary_authoring\",\n    \"challenger_authoring\",\n    \"evidence_judgment\",\n    \"adversarial_judgment\",\n    \"consensus_arbitration\",\n    \"change_intelligence\",\n    \"goldset_curation\",\n    # E14 — REMOVED, and deliberately not replaced: `implementation_review`,\n    # `architectural_arbitration`, `auth_security_review`,\n    # `access_boundary_review`, `tenant_isolation_review`. Plan 019 Phase 2.5\n    # added them as a contract extension for lanes that were then built\n    # differently: the auth lane became the security-boundary ADAPTER plus the\n    # architecture spine gate, and domain review became `specialist_domain_review`\n    # (specialist_review_runner's touch-map) plus expert_review_gate. Five roles\n    # a request could name, that no kernel path ever minted and no bridge ever\n    # consumed — a surface that admits a role nothing can fulfil is how a caller\n    # ends up waiting forever for an answer that was never dispatched. Removal is\n    # pinned by tests/test_role_hygiene_e14.py; ORPHAN-MEDIUM-280 closed the same\n    # defect from the agent-prompt side (a prompt claiming `implementation_review`\n    # the kernel never routes).\n    # ORPHAN-HIGH-426 — independent adjudication of a HUMAN_REQUIRED\n    # escalation. Paired with THREE distinct judge agents below so a panel\n    # is composed of distinct principals by construction rather than by\n    # hope; the fold still verifies disjointness against the claims ledger.\n    \"human_required_adjudication\",\n)\n\nINVOCATION_ROLES: FrozenSet[str] = frozenset({\n    *REQUEST_ROLES,\n    \"specialist_domain_review\",\n})\n\nDISPATCHABLE_ROLES: FrozenSet[str] = frozenset({\n    \"specialist_domain_review\",\n    \"primary_authoring\",\n    \"challenger_authoring\",\n    \"evidence_judgment\",\n    \"adversarial_judgment\",\n    \"primary_plan\",\n    \"challenger_plan\",\n    \"cross_review\",\n    \"completeness_critique\",\n    \"implementation\",\n    \"human_required_adjudication\",\n    # E14 — the three roles that gained a producer. A minted envelope whose\n    # role the executor refuses to claim (`ci_executor.claim_and_dispatch_one`\n    # validates against this set) is a request that waits forever: the mint\n    # would look alive in the ledger and be dead on the lane. Minting and\n    # draining are one contract, so a role joins both sides together.\n    \"consensus_arbitration\",\n    \"change_intelligence\",\n    \"goldset_curation\",\n    # E9-c — adversarial re-review of a decision the pipeline already closed.\n    # Added together with the minter in `decision_questioning`, never before\n    # it: a role that can be dispatched but is never minted, and a role that\n    # is minted but can never be claimed, are the same defect from opposite\n    # ends, and this repository has now closed nineteen of them.\n    \"verification\",\n})\n\nDRAFTER_ROLES: FrozenSet[str] = frozenset({\n    \"primary_authoring\",\n    \"challenger_authoring\",\n})\n\nJUDGE_ROLES: tuple[str, ...] = (\n    \"evidence_judgment\",\n    \"adversarial_judgment\",\n    \"consensus_arbitration\",\n)\n\nSUPPORTING_ROLES: tuple[str, ...] = (\n    \"change_intelligence\",\n    \"goldset_curation\",\n)\n\nPLANNER_BRIDGE_ROLES: FrozenSet[str] = frozenset({\n    \"primary_plan\",\n    \"challenger_plan\",\n    \"cross_review\",\n    \"implementation\",\n})\n\nBRIDGE_REQUIRED_ROLES: FrozenSet[str] = frozenset({\n    *JUDGE_ROLES,\n    *SUPPORTING_ROLES,\n    *PLANNER_BRIDGE_ROLES,\n})\n\nDEFAULT_TARGET_AGENT_WHITELIST: tuple[str, ...] = (\n    \"aria-prompt-writer\",\n    \"aria-primary-planner\",","numLines":120,"startLine":1,"totalLines":252}}}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CfQ2Z4HQbKV3uwa4YrgTo","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"```json\n{\n  \"$schema\": \"aria/agent-response/v1\",\n  \"request_id\": \"AIR-aria-consensus-arbiter-00bb0e8ea72e\",\n  \"claim_id\": \"AIR-aria-consensus-arbiter-00bb0e8ea72e\",\n  \"agent_id\": \"aria-consensus-arbiter\",\n  \"role\": \"consensus_arbitration\",\n  \"status\": \"submitted\",\n  \"output_path\": \"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/.aria-state-store/tools/agent-invocations/outputs/general/round-na-consensus_arbitration-AIR-aria-consensus-arbiter-00bb0e8ea72e.md\",\n  \"satisfaction_matrix\": [\n    {\n      \"id\": \"consensus\",\n      \"verdict\": \"satisfied\",\n      \"note\": \"Judged the finding myself from the repository rather than aggregating: read the profile row, then hunted its readers, then the one agent file that names it, then the kernel dispatch whitelist that decides whether the row is ever selected for a spawn. My own verdict is false_positive at 0.86 — the row has consumers keyed on an agent-markdown reference, so an operator cannot edit or delete it without effect. The prior pair's verdict therefore survives, and I record one asymmetry neither of them stated: this is the single profile whose enforcement derivations never run.\",\n      \"evidence_refs\": [\n        \"aria-kernel/aria_kernel/data/runtime_profiles.json:4\",\n        \"aria-kernel/aria_kernel/data/runtime_profiles.json:18\",\n        \"aria-kernel/aria_kernel/data/runtime_profiles.json:19\",\n        \"aria-kernel/aria_kernel/data/runtime_profiles.json:22\"\n      ]\n    }\n  ],\n  \"evidence_refs\": [\n    \"aria-kernel/aria_kernel/data/runtime_profiles.json:4\",\n    \"aria-kernel/aria_kernel/data/runtime_profiles.json:18\",\n    \"aria-kernel/aria_kernel/data/runtime_profiles.json:19\",\n    \"aria-kernel/aria_kernel/data/runtime_profiles.json:22\",\n    \"aria-kernel/aria_kernel/data/runtime_profiles.json\"\n  ],\n  \"details\": {\n    \"consensus\": {\n      \"verdict\": \"false_positive\",\n      \"mean_confidence\": 0.81,\n      \"judge_count\": 3,\n      \"gate\": \"pass\",\n      \"judge_count_basis\": \"Two prior judges plus this arbiter's independent verdict; all three land on false_positive, so all three are counted as agreeing with the settled verdict. Mean of 0.65, 0.92 and 0.86 is 0.81, above the 0.80 bar.\",\n      \"mode\": \"anchor_refutation\",\n      \"refutation_attempted\": true,\n      \"refuted\": false,\n      \"prior_verdicts_survive\": true,\n      \"arbiter_verdict\": \"false_positive\",\n      \"arbiter_confidence\": 0.86,\n      \"tool_id\": \"kernel-dead-wire-adapter\",\n      \"run_id\": \"35c50d01-df5c-45eb-a285-854f1ccadda0\",\n      \"finding_id\": \"kernel-dead-wire:policy:aria-kernel/aria_kernel/data/runtime_profiles.json:planner_orchestrator\",\n      \"judgment_group_id\": \"judge:kernel-dead-wire-adapter:finding:7b9c599aa82a30185834854168d9733a38328d78a26fdc56c7b09a2108b392ad\",\n      \"judges\": [\n        {\"judge_id\": \"aria-adversarial-judge\", \"verdict\": \"false_positive\", \"confidence\": 0.65, \"agreed_with_settled_verdict\": true},\n        {\"judge_id\": \"aria-evidence-judge\", \"verdict\": \"false_positive\", \"confidence\": 0.92, \"agreed_with_settled_verdict\": true},\n        {\"judge_id\": \"aria-consensus-arbiter\", \"verdict\": \"false_positive\", \"confidence\": 0.86, \"agreed_with_settled_verdict\": true}\n      ],\n      \"evidence\": [\n        \"aria-kernel/aria_kernel/data/runtime_profiles.json:4\",\n        \"aria-kernel/aria_kernel/data/runtime_profiles.json:18\",\n        \"aria-kernel/aria_kernel/data/runtime_profiles.json:19\",\n        \"aria-kernel/aria_kernel/data/runtime_profiles.json:22\",\n        \"aria-kernel/aria_kernel/data/runtime_profiles.json\"\n      ],\n      \"rationale\": \"The finding asserts a policy row nobody consumes — a key an operator could retune or delete without effect. I tested that claim by reading the row and then hunting its readers, and it does not hold. The row is at runtime_profiles.json:18-29; its description (line 19) declares a read-only lead that may dispatch sub-agents, and line 22 grants Read, Grep, Glob and Agent, which makes it the only profile in the file that grants Agent at all. The file's own header (line 4) states its standing: this file is the authority for what an agent may run as, agent markdown REFERENCES a profile by id and MIRRORS model/effort/tools, and a mirror that disagrees fails validation. Both readers of the row are keyed on that markdown reference rather than on a Python literal, which is exactly why a literal scan of production Python sees nothing. Reader one: aria-kernel/aria_kernel/agent_runtime_profile.py parses runtime_profile out of the frontmatter and looks the id up in the loaded map (lines 219-242), returning the kernel's model, effort, tools, write_scope, env_passthrough and external_writes as the resolved envelope, and falling back to an empty-tool default_invalid envelope when the id is unknown. Reader two: aria-kernel/aria_kernel/runtime_profiles.py::verify_agent_mirrors (lines 234-272) globs .claude/agents/aria-*.md and emits a defect row for any mirror that disagrees with the kernel. And the referent exists: .claude/agents/aria-acceptance-lead.md:3 names runtime_profile: planner_orchestrator, with lines 5-7 mirroring it exactly (opus, max, Read/Grep/Glob/Agent). So an edit is consequential in both directions — retune model, effort or tools in the JSON alone and the mirror check reports drift against that file; delete the row and that agent's envelope resolves to default_invalid with no tools. A key whose edit is caught and whose deletion changes a resolved envelope is not a dead tunable, so the dead-wire claim is a false positive.\",\n      \"nuance_the_finding_is_right_about\": \"There is a real asymmetry under the finding, and it is worth recording rather than suppressing. This is the one profile whose ENFORCEMENT is never exercised. Its only referent carries dispatch: ad-hoc (aria-acceptance-lead.md:9), and aria-acceptance-lead appears in neither agent_surface.DEFAULT_TARGET_AGENT_WHITELIST (lines 118-142) nor ROLE_TARGET_PAIRING (lines 144-166), so no kernel mint ever targets it; the derivations that give a profile teeth on the CLI spawn path — disallowed_tools_for, the env_passthrough spawn environment, the write_scope sandbox bind — are therefore never computed for this row. On the interactive lane the Claude Code loader reads the frontmatter mirror instead. The row is live as the authority a mirror is checked against and inert as a spawn envelope. Those are two different claims, and only the first is the one a dead-wire verdict decides.\",\n      \"residual_uncertainty\": \"This route had a file reader and no search tool, so I could not enumerate callers of verify_agent_mirrors. Two facts bound that gap. Its docstring claims it is used by the Plan 032 invariant test and available to doctor, and I checked the doctor: run_doctor's organ registry at aria-kernel/aria_kernel/doctor.py:603-641 contains no mirror check, so 'available to' is not wiring and the doctor is not the caller — a prose-versus-wiring gap that deserves its own process observation. Separately, verify_agent_mirrors carries no entry in aria-kernel/control-reachability.dormant.json, which the reachability gate requires of any verify_-prefixed control with no production reference. Neither branch changes this verdict: if the invariant test is the only consumer, the repository's own recorded disposition (that same waiver file's validate_file entry, lines 14-18) is that an invariant test is a control's intended and sufficient consumer, and the agent_runtime_profile resolution path reads the row independently of the verifier.\",\n      \"what_would_flip_this_verdict\": \"Remove the runtime_profile: planner_orchestrator line from .claude/agents/aria-acceptance-lead.md, or retire that agent, and no aria-*.md would name the row; both readers would then skip it and the finding would become true. The row's liveness is exactly one frontmatter line deep. That fragility is an observation to record against the profile file, not grounds to call the wire dead today.\"\n    },\n    \"teaching\": {\n      \"what_must_be_done\": \"Anchor arbitration is not aggregation. Two judges agreed, so the task was to re-judge the finding from the repository and try to break it: read the flagged key, then find every reader that consumes it, then check whether any of those readers actually runs — and only then say whether the pair's verdict survives.\",\n      \"why_it_matters\": \"A verdict two judges agree on is about to become repository ground truth. A false_positive at anchor grade suppresses this finding class for kernel-dead-wire-adapter and scores the judges who produced it, so the pair's agreement stops being a claim and starts being a rule that silences future reports.\",\n      \"what_breaks_if_it_is_skipped\": \"A ratifying third judge converts an unexamined agreement into a suppression rule. If the pair had been wrong here, the adapter would lose the ability to report a genuinely dead policy row, and the next operator to edit runtime_profiles.json would get no signal. A rubber stamp promoted to ground truth is worse than having no third judge, because it carries the authority of independent confirmation without the work.\",\n      \"downstream_surface\": \"feedback_store.generate_ai_consensus reads details.consensus and counts only the judges who agreed with the settled verdict; that count drives finding suppression, rule quarantine and judge calibration scores. Refusing to back a verdict keeps it below anchor grade — which is why disagreement is a consequential answer here and not an abstention.\",\n      \"what_evidence_proves_the_result\": \"A dead-wire claim is settled by the reader chain, not by prose. The proof is: the declared row (runtime_profiles.json:18-29 with the Agent grant at line 22 and the authority statement at line 4), one agent file naming it in frontmatter, two kernel readers that look the id up from that frontmatter, and the observable consequence of an edit — mirror drift reported for that file, or an empty resolved envelope if the row is deleted. I tested that claim by reading the row and then hunting its readers, and it does not hold. The row is at runtime_profiles.json:18-29; its description (line 19) declares a read-only lead that may dispatch sub-agents, and line 22 grants Read, Grep, Glob and Agent, which makes it the only profile in the file that grants Agent at all. The file's own header (line 4) states its standing: this file is the authority for what an agent may run as, agent markdown REFERENCES a profile by id and MIRRORS model/effort/tools, and a mirror that disagrees fails validation. Both readers of the row are keyed on that markdown reference rather than on a Python literal, which is exactly why a literal scan of production Python sees nothing. Reader one: aria-kernel/aria_kernel/agent_runtime_profile.py parses runtime_profile out of the frontmatter and looks the id up in the loaded map (lines 219-242), returning the kernel's model, effort, tools, write_scope, env_passthrough and external_writes as the resolved envelope, and falling back to an empty-tool default_invalid envelope when the id is unknown. Reader two: aria-kernel/aria_kernel/runtime_profiles.py::verify_agent_mirrors (lines 234-272) globs .claude/agents/aria-*.md and emits a defect row for any mirror that disagrees with the kernel. And the referent exists: .claude/agents/aria-acceptance-lead.md:3 names runtime_profile: planner_orchestrator, with lines 5-7 mirroring it exactly (opus, max, Read/Grep/Glob/Agent). So an edit is consequential in both directions — retune model, effort or tools in the JSON alone and the mirror check reports drift against that file; delete the row and that agent's envelope resolves to default_invalid with no tools. A key whose edit is caught and whose deletion changes a resolved envelope is not a dead tunable, so the dead-wire claim is a false positive.\",\n      \"nuance_the_finding_is_right_about\": \"There is a real asymmetry under the finding, and it is worth recording rather than suppressing. This is the one profile whose ENFORCEMENT is never exercised. Its only referent carries dispatch: ad-hoc (aria-acceptance-lead.md:9), and aria-acceptance-lead appears in neither agent_surface.DEFAULT_TARGET_AGENT_WHITELIST (lines 118-142) nor ROLE_TARGET_PAIRING (lines 144-166), so no kernel mint ever targets it; the derivations that give a profile teeth on the CLI spawn path — disallowed_tools_for, the env_passthrough spawn environment, the write_scope sandbox bind — are therefore never computed for this row. On the interactive lane the Claude Code loader reads the frontmatter mirror instead. The row is live as the authority a mirror is checked against and inert as a spawn envelope. Those are two different claims, and only the first is the one a dead-wire verdict decides.\",\n      \"residual_uncertainty\": \"This route had a file reader and no search tool, so I could not enumerate callers of verify_agent_mirrors. Two facts bound that gap. Its docstring claims it is used by the Plan 032 invariant test and available to doctor, and I checked the doctor: run_doctor's organ registry at aria-kernel/aria_kernel/doctor.py:603-641 contains no mirror check, so 'available to' is not wiring and the doctor is not the caller — a prose-versus-wiring gap that deserves its own process observation. Separately, verify_agent_mirrors carries no entry in aria-kernel/control-reachability.dormant.json, which the reachability gate requires of any verify_-prefixed control with no production reference. Neither branch changes this verdict: if the invariant test is the only consumer, the repository's own recorded disposition (that same waiver file's validate_file entry, lines 14-18) is that an invariant test is a control's intended and sufficient consumer, and the agent_runtime_profile resolution path reads the row independently of the verifier.\",\n      \"what_would_flip_this_verdict\": \"Remove the runtime_profile: planner_orchestrator line from .claude/agents/aria-acceptance-lead.md, or retire that agent, and no aria-*.md would name the row; both readers would then skip it and the finding would become true. The row's liveness is exactly one frontmatter line deep. That fragility is an observation to record against the profile file, not grounds to call the wire dead today.\"\n    },\n    \"teaching\": {\n      \"what_must_be_done\": \"Anchor arbitration is not aggregation. Two judges agreed, so the task was to re-judge the finding from the repository and try to break it: read the flagged key, then find every reader that consumes it, then check whether any of those readers actually runs — and only then say whether the pair's verdict survives.\",\n      \"why_it_matters\": \"A verdict two judges agree on is about to become repository ground truth. A false_positive at anchor grade suppresses this finding class for kernel-dead-wire-adapter and scores the judges who produced it, so the pair's agreement stops being a claim and starts being a rule that silences future reports.\",\n      \"what_breaks_if_it_is_skipped\": \"A ratifying third judge converts an unexamined agreement into a suppression rule.
